# Execution Plan — `--json` output mode + `--dry-run` UX

Context gathered: `synccli.py:72` (`sync()`), `synccli.py:90` (`main()`), `incremental.py` imports `copy_file` (internal caller), existing tests in `test_synccli.py`. Note: `test_first_item_stale_is_included` appears to already fail (`collect_stale` loop starts at index 1, `synccli.py:55`) — must be confirmed as pre-existing, not caused by this change.

---

**Task 1 — Baseline exploration**
- **Agent:** explore
- **Objective:** Run `python3 -m unittest -v` and record which tests pass/fail *before* any change; enumerate all callers of `sync()`, `main()`, `copy_file` (incl. `incremental.py`); confirm no other module prints to stdout.
- **Files:** none (read-only).
- **Risks:** None; skipping it risks misattributing the pre-existing `collect_stale` test failure to this change.
- **Acceptance:** Written baseline: exact failing-test list + caller inventory.

**Task 2 — Design review of the approach (before code)**
- **Agent:** design-review (cheap-design-review; escalate to strong-design-review only if it flags a real contract issue)
- **Objective:** Ratify a 5-line design note: (a) `--json` handled entirely in `main()` — `sync()` untouched, signature stable; (b) `skipped` semantics defined as `len(scan_files(src)) - len(copied)` (counting unreadable files silently dropped by `copy_file`, `synccli.py:68`, is out of scope — `sync()` can't report them without a signature change); (c) stdout purity: any error/warning output goes to stderr; (d) exit code path unchanged (`return 0`).
- **Files:** none (text-only verdict).
- **Risks:** Ambiguous `skipped` definition (GOAL.md doesn't define it); tempted "fix" of `sync()` return type would break `incremental.py` and the contract.
- **Acceptance:** Explicit verdict on (b) and (c) recorded; no open questions.

**Task 3 — Implementation**
- **Agent:** impl (cheap-code)
- **Objective:** Add `--json` flag to the argparse parser in `main()`; in JSON mode print exactly one `json.dumps({"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...})` to stdout and nothing else; with `--dry-run`, `copied` = the list `sync(dry_run=True)` returns (would-be copies); human mode (one path per line) byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py` (only `main()`; do not touch `sync()` or below).
- **Risks:** Accidental extra stdout output (argparse errors already go to stderr — fine); changing `sync()` signature; `skipped` computed from a second scan racing with the first (acceptable: compute from the same `src` dir).
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m synccli SRC DST --json` emits exactly one line, `json.loads`-able, with the 4 keys; `--json --dry-run` lists would-be copies and modifies nothing in DST; human mode diff-free.

**Task 4 — Tests**
- **Agent:** impl (cheap-code, same session/task_id as Task 3 — small delta, context already loaded)
- **Objective:** Add unit tests to `test_synccli.py`: (1) `--json` stdout parses as JSON and has exactly the keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`; (2) stdout contains nothing but that object (capture via `contextlib.redirect_stdout`); (3) `--json --dry-run`: `copied` lists pending files, DST unchanged, no manifest written; (4) human mode still prints one path per line; (5) exit code 0 in both modes.
- **Files to modify:** `test_synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Tests coupled to tmpdir paths (`src`/`dst` values) being brittle; forgetting the dry-run no-manifest assertion.
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m unittest -v` — all *new* tests pass; the only failure (if any) is the Task-1-confirmed pre-existing `collect_stale` one.

**Task 5 — Mandatory review**
- **Agent:** review (cheap-review; escalate major findings to strong-code, then strong-code-alternative)
- **Objective:** Review the Task 3+4 diff: `sync()` signature untouched, `incremental.py` still imports cleanly, stdout purity holds on all code paths (including `sync()` raising — no partial JSON), no new dependencies, Python 3.10 stdlib only, no comments/style drift.
- **Files:** read-only (diff of `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`).
- **Risks:** Reviewer scope-creeping into the pre-existing `collect_stale`/`filter_excluded` mutable-default bugs — classify those as out-of-scope observations, not blockers.
- **Acceptance:** Verdict with zero major findings; minor findings fixed by resuming the Task 3 cheap-code `task_id`.

**Task 6 — Final verification**
- **Agent:** impl (resume Task 3 task_id) runs it; verdict stays with review
- **Objective:** Run `python3 -m unittest -v` plus a manual smoke: real `--json`, `--json --dry-run`, and plain runs against temp dirs.
- **Files:** none.
- **Risks:** None.
- **Acceptance:** Full suite green except the documented pre-existing failure; smoke outputs match GOAL.md §1–3 verbatim.

# Execution Plan: JSON Output Mode + Dry-Run UX (`synccli`)

## Context & Constraints (from `GOAL.md` and `README.md`)
- **Goal**: Add a `--json` flag to `synccli` that outputs a single machine-readable JSON object to `stdout`: `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}` with no extraneous `stdout` output and unchanged exit code (`0`).
- **Interplay with `--dry-run`**: When `--json` and `--dry-run` are combined, `"copied"` lists the relative paths that *would* be copied (without mutating `dst` or writing `.sync_manifest.json`).
- **Backwards Compatibility**: Default human-readable CLI output (one relative path per line) and exit code behavior must remain unchanged.
- **Technical Constraints**:
  - Python 3.10 standard library only (`argparse`, `json`, `os`, `sys`, `unittest`, etc.); no new third-party dependencies.
  - Keep `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature and return contract (`list[str]`) stable for existing internal callers and tests.
- **Verification Command**: `python3 -m unittest -v` and `python3 -m synccli --help`.

---

## Ordered Tasks

### Task 1: Codebase Exploration & Baseline Audit
- **Objective**: Inspect `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py` to map all callers of `sync()` and `main()`, verify how `scan_files()` / `build_manifest()` / `changed_files()` compute candidate files versus copied files, and establish how `skipped` should be derived without breaking `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)`.
- **Files to create/modify**: None (read-only inspection of `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, `README.md`).
- **Pipeline agent**: `explore` (`explore` subagent)
- **Risks**:
  - Changing `sync()`'s return value (e.g., returning a `(copied, skipped)` tuple or dict) would break existing internal callers and `TestSync` unit tests.
  - Pre-existing unrelated test failures in `test_synccli.py` (such as `TestCollectStale.test_first_item_stale_is_included`) could obscure new test results if not identified upfront.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion**: Written summary confirming `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False) -> list[str]` callers, exact formula for `skipped` (`len(scan_files(src_root)) - len(copied)`), and current baseline output of `python3 -m unittest -v`.

---

### Task 2: Design Review of `--json` Output Contract & `skipped` Semantics
- **Objective**: Validate the technical design for computing `"skipped"` and formatting the JSON payload in `main()` while preserving `sync()`'s signature and return type. Confirm edge-case semantics:
  1. First sync (`skipped == 0`, `copied == [...]`).
  2. Subsequent no-op sync (`skipped == total_src_files`, `copied == []`).
  3. Partial update (`skipped == total_src_files - len(copied)`).
  4. `--json --dry-run` (`copied` contains files that would be copied, `skipped` counts unchanged files, no files or `.sync_manifest.json` written to `dst`).
  5. `src` and `dst` values in JSON match the passed CLI arguments (`args.src`, `args.dst`).
- **Files to create/modify**: None (read-only design critique against `GOAL.md` and `synccli.py`).
- **Pipeline agent**: `design-review` (`cheap-design-review` subagent)
- **Risks**:
  - Re-scanning `src` after `sync()` vs. computing file counts cleanly could diverge if not derived consistently via `scan_files(args.src)` (which excludes hidden directories starting with `.` like `.sync_manifest.json`).
  - Accidental printing of human-mode lines or logs to `stdout` when `--json` is active, violating the single-JSON-object requirement.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion**: Design sign-off confirming:
  - `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature and `list[str]` return value remain untouched.
  - `main(argv=None)` adds `parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")` and branches cleanly between `--json` (`print(json.dumps({"copied": copied, "skipped": skipped, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst}))`) and default human output.

---

### Task 3: Implement `--json` Flag and `--dry-run` Interplay in `synccli.py`
- **Objective**: Update `main()` in `synccli.py` to accept `--json`, compute `skipped` (`len(scan_files(args.src)) - len(copied)`), and emit a single JSON object `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst}` to `stdout` when `--json` is set, while keeping human output mode and exit code (`0`) unchanged.
- **Files to create/modify**:
  - Modify: `synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent**: `impl` (`cheap-code` subagent)
- **Risks**:
  - Breaking `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature or return type for existing callers.
  - Printing extra lines or trailing output alongside the JSON object when `copied` is non-empty.
  - Modifying destination files or `.sync_manifest.json` when `--dry-run` is combined with `--json`.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion**:
  - `python3 -m synccli --help` shows both `--dry-run` and `--json`.
  - Running `synccli.main([src, dst, "--json"])` prints valid JSON parsable by `json.loads()` with exact keys `{"copied", "skipped", "src", "dst"}` and returns `0`.
  - Running `synccli.main([src, dst, "--dry-run", "--json"])` reports the files that would be copied in `"copied"`, leaves `dst` untouched, and returns `0`.
  - Running `synccli.main([src, dst])` (without `--json`) still prints one relative path per line.

---

### Task 4: Add Unit Tests for JSON Mode and Dry-Run Interplay
- **Objective**: Add unit tests in `test_synccli.py` covering `--json` output validity, required keys and types (`copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`), `skipped` count accuracy across initial and repeat syncs, `--json` + `--dry-run` interplay, and preservation of human mode output and exit codes.
- **Files to create/modify**:
  - Modify: `test_synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent**: `impl` (`cheap-code` / `cheap-mech` subagent, resuming the implementation session)
- **Risks**:
  - Not capturing `sys.stdout` (`io.StringIO` / `unittest.mock.patch("sys.stdout")` / `contextlib.redirect_stdout`) accurately to verify that *nothing else* is printed to `stdout` in `--json` mode.
  - Leaving temporary directories uncleaned or coupling test state across test methods.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion**:
  - `python3 -m unittest test_synccli.TestSync -v` (and the new JSON/CLI test case class in `test_synccli.py`) passes 100% with tests explicitly asserting:
    1. `json.loads(stdout)` succeeds on a single stdout payload.
    2. Exact keys `{"copied", "skipped", "src", "dst"}` are present with expected values (`copied == ["a.txt"]`, `skipped == 0` on first run; `copied == []`, `skipped == 1` on second run).
    3. `--json` + `--dry-run` populates `"copied": ["a.txt"]`, `"skipped": 0`, does not copy files or write `.sync_manifest.json`, and returns exit code `0`.
    4. Human mode (`main([src, dst])`) still outputs one path per line.

---

### Task 5: Code & Test Review (`GOAL.md` Compliance Check)
- **Objective**: Review the diff in `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py` against all requirements and constraints in `GOAL.md` and `README.md`.
- **Files to create/modify**: None (read-only review of `git diff` on `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; any minor findings routed back to `impl`).
- **Pipeline agent**: `review` (`cheap-review` subagent)
- **Risks**:
  - Subtle deviation from `GOAL.md` (e.g., extra keys in JSON, altered `sync()` signature, non-stdlib imports, or incomplete test coverage of `--dry-run` + `--json`).
  - Unintended edits outside `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion**:
  - `cheap-review` reports zero major/minor defects.
  - `git diff --name-only` lists only `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`.
  - All `TestSync` and JSON CLI unit tests pass via `python3 -m unittest -v`.

# Execution Plan — `--json` flag + dry-run UX

## Current state (read-only recon)
- `synccli.py:90` `main()` prints one path per line; `sync(src, dst, dry_run)` (synccli.py:72) already returns the copied list and honors dry-run — signature can stay stable.
- `test_synccli.py` has `TestSync`; no JSON/CLI tests yet.
- Constraints from GOAL.md: stdlib-only, no new deps, `sync()` signature stable.

---

## Task 1 — Explore: confirm all internal callers of `sync()`
- **Objective**: Verify `sync()` call sites (e.g. future `syncd` per DESIGN.md) so the signature stays untouched and output contract is clear.
- **Agent**: `explore`
- **Files**: none modified; report only.
- **Risks**: none (read-only).
- **Acceptance**: Report lists every caller of `sync()` and confirms whether any parse `main()`'s stdout.

## Task 2 — Design review: JSON contract shape
- **Objective**: Decide exact contract: `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}`; `skipped` definition (GOAL.md doesn't define it — likely count of unchanged files = len(manifest) − len(copied), needs a decision); stdout purity in JSON mode (argparse errors go to stderr already; ensure no warnings land on stdout).
- **Agent**: `design-review`
- **Files**: none modified; verdict on the contract above.
- **Risks**: ambiguous `skipped` semantics; `src`/`dst` should be the literal CLI args (not resolved paths) — confirm.
- **Acceptance**: Written verdict resolving `skipped` semantics and `src`/`dst` values, accepted before implementation.

## Task 3 — Implement `--json` in `main()`
- **Objective**: Add `--json` flag; in JSON mode print exactly one `json.dumps` object to stdout and nothing else; human mode byte-identical to today; exit code stays 0.
- **Agent**: `cheap-code` (impl)
- **Files**: modify `synccli.py` (only `main()`; `sync()` untouched — it already returns the list needed for `copied`; `skipped` derived in `main()`).
- **Risks**: accidentally printing to stdout in JSON mode; changing `sync()` signature; adding non-stdlib deps.
- **Acceptance**: `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` stdout parses as JSON with all 4 keys; `python3 -m synccli src dst` output unchanged; `python3 -m unittest` green.

## Task 4 — Review the diff
- **Agent**: `cheap-review`
- **Files**: none modified; findings on Task 3's diff.
- **Risks**: reviewer missing stdout-purity edge cases (e.g. buffering, argparse `--help`).
- **Acceptance**: Findings classified minor/major; zero majors outstanding before Task 5.

## Task 5 — Tests in `test_synccli.py`
- **Objective**: New `TestJsonOutput` covering: valid JSON parse; all 4 keys present with right types; dry-run mode (`--dry-run --json`) lists would-be copies and dst untouched; human mode unchanged; exit code 0 in both modes.
- **Agent**: `cheap-code` (resume same task_id as Task 3 — minor-fix iteration)
- **Files**: modify `test_synccli.py` (add test class only).
- **Risks**: mtime flakiness in dry-run assertions; test depending on cwd.
- **Acceptance**: `python3 -m unittest -v` passes; new tests fail on a build with the flag reverted (proves coverage).

## Task 6 — Docs + final audit
- **Objective**: Document `--json` in README.md usage section.
- **Agents**: `cheap-mech` (README one-liner), then `strong-review-alternative` (final audit — cheap-code produced most of the work).
- **Files**: modify `README.md`.
- **Risks**: doc drift vs. actual behavior.
- **Acceptance**: README shows both flags; full suite green; auditor signs off on stdout purity + stable `sync()` signature.

+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged

Both confirmed empirically. Defects in `incremental.py`:

**Major**

1. **`filter_excluded` — mutable default accumulator** (incremental.py:10). `excluded=[]` persists across calls, so every call returns all previously-excluded paths plus new ones. Verified: a second call with only `ok.txt` returns `['.git/config']` from the first call. Corrupted results for any caller invoking it more than once per process.

2. **`copy_many` — reports failed copies as done** (incremental.py:18-27). `synccli.copy_file` swallows `OSError` internally (synccli.py:68-69), so an unreadable/missing source never raises — `copy_many` appends `dst` to `done` anyway. Verified: a nonexistent source still appears in the returned "done" list. The contract ("list of dst paths done") is violated, and a caller treating `done` as "synced" (e.g., recording it in a manifest) would silently mark uncopied files as synced — data loss. The `except Exception: continue` additionally discards which pairs failed with no error channel at all.

**Minor**

3. **`merge_manifest` — `KeyError` on entries without `"mtime"`** (incremental.py:38). `old[rel]["mtime"]` assumes every legacy entry has the key; a manifest entry missing it (older/corrupt manifest) crashes the whole merge instead of treating it as replaceable.

4. **`filter_excluded` misses nested `__pycache__`** (incremental.py:13). Only the first path segment is checked, so `pkg/__pycache__/x.pyc` — the common case — passes through the exclusion filter. (`EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS` suggests top-level-only is deliberate, but for `__pycache__` that makes the exclusion largely ineffective.)

Not defects: `merge_manifest` keeping entries absent from `new_manifest` is consistent with "merge" semantics, and returning the dict without writing is consistent with the docstring.

"""synccli: synchronize a source directory into a destination using a manifest."""
import argparse
import json
import os
import sys

MANIFEST_NAME = ".sync_manifest.json"


def scan_files(root):
    """Return sorted list of relative file paths under root."""
    result = []
    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
        dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if not d.startswith(".")]
        for name in filenames:
            full = os.path.join(dirpath, name)
            rel = os.path.relpath(full, root)
            result.append(rel)
    return sorted(result)


def file_state(path):
    """Return (size, mtime) for a file."""
    st = os.stat(path)
    return {"size": st.st_size, "mtime": st.st_mtime}


def build_manifest(root):
    """Build a manifest {relpath: state} for all files under root."""
    manifest = {}
    for rel in scan_files(root):
        manifest[rel] = file_state(os.path.join(root, rel))
    return manifest


def changed_files(old_manifest, new_manifest):
    """Return files whose state changed or that are new in new_manifest."""
    changed = []
    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
        if rel not in old_manifest or old_manifest[rel] != state:
            changed.append(rel)
    return changed


def collect_stale(items, seen=None):
    """Collect stale entries: items not yet marked fresh.

    Args:
        items: iterable of (path, fresh) tuples.
        seen: set of paths already marked fresh; defaults to a fresh set.
[0m
[0m• [0mReview HEAD commit in incremental.py[90m Cheap-Review Agent[0m
**Real defects in `incremental.py` (commit fe8ce2c):**

1. **Major — `copy_many` silently swallows copy failures** (`incremental.py:29-31`). `except Exception: continue` drops every error; the returned `done` list is indistinguishable from "everything succeeded". A failed copy (disk full, permission denied) means files missing at destination with no signal — silent data loss in a sync tool. At minimum collect and return/report failures.

2. **Major — mutable default argument in `filter_excluded`** (`incremental.py:14`). `excluded=[]` is created once at function definition, so results accumulate across calls: a second call returns paths excluded in the *first* call, i.e. paths that aren't in its `paths` input at all — corrupted, misleading return value. (It also mutates a caller-passed list as a hidden side effect.)

3. **Minor — `merge_manifest` never drops entries deleted from the source** (`incremental.py:40`). Files removed at the source keep their old entry forever, so the destination manifest grows stale and no longer reflects reality; if the file is later recreated with a matching mtime, the merge can wrongly skip recording the new state. Deletions should propagate (e.g. drop keys absent from `new_manifest`).

### Defects in `HEAD` (`83a417f` — `incremental.py`)

1. **[Major] `filter_excluded` (`incremental.py:10-15`) — Mutable default argument corrupts results across calls**
   - `def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):` mutates the default list in place via `excluded.append(p)`. Subsequent calls without an explicit `excluded` argument accumulate and return results from all prior calls. Passing a custom list also mutates the caller's list as an undocumented side effect.

2. **[Major] `copy_many` (`incremental.py:18-27`) — Failed file copies are falsely reported as succeeded (silent data loss)**
   - `copy_many` relies on `copy_file(src, dst)` raising an exception on failure before executing `done.append(dst)`. However, `synccli.copy_file` (`synccli.py:65-69`) catches `OSError` internally (`except OSError: pass`) and returns normally when opening/reading `src` or opening/writing `dst` fails.
   - As a result, `copy_many` appends `dst` to `done` even when the file was never copied or was truncated, causing callers to treat failed transfers as completed.

3. **[Major] `merge_manifest` (`incremental.py:30-40`) — Misleading contract (never writes to `dst_manifest_path`) and inconsistent state merge**
   - **No persistence:** Despite accepting a file path `dst_manifest_path` and stating *"Merge new file states into the destination manifest"*, the function only reads `dst_manifest_path` and returns the dict without writing the merged manifest back to disk.
   - **State mismatch with `synccli.changed_files`:** `synccli.changed_files` triggers a sync whenever `old_manifest[rel] != state` (comparing both `size` and `mtime`), whereas `merge_manifest` only updates entries when `old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]`. If a file is reverted/restored to an older `mtime` (even with a different `size`), `synccli` copies it, but `merge_manifest` retains the old manifest entry, leaving the manifest out of sync with the destination filesystem and causing repeated re-copies on every subsequent run.

4. **[Minor] `copy_many` (`incremental.py:25-26`) — Broad `except Exception: continue` silently hides failures**
   - Any exception raised outside `copy_file`'s `try` block (such as `os.makedirs` failing on permissions or an invalid path in `synccli.py:64`) is silently swallowed without logging or surfacing failed pairs to the caller.

5. **[Minor] `filter_excluded` (`incremental.py:7, 12-14`) — Brittle path matching misses nested excluded directories and non-normalized paths**
   - `p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS` only checks the first segment of `p` split by `os.sep`. It fails to match nested `__pycache__` directories (e.g., `pkg/__pycache__/a.pyc`), paths prefixed with `./`, or paths using `/` on Windows (`os.altsep`). Additionally, `.sync_manifest.json` is a file, not a directory as `EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS` implies.

[0m• [0mCritique DESIGN.md approach[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
**Critical gaps**

1. **Deletions are never handled.** `changed_files` (synccli.py:36) only reports new/changed files — files deleted from `src` stay in `dst` forever. A "continuous sync" that never propagates deletes is the #1 functional hole; the design doesn't mention it at all.

2. **"Atomicity is not needed" is wrong.** A daemon gets killed mid-write constantly. Partial JSON → `json.load` crash on next boot (synccli.py:79) → either daemon won't start or triggers a full resync. Write-to-temp + `os.replace` is one line; skipping it is choosing corruption under the exact conditions a daemon sees.

3. **Lost changes on mid-copy edits.** Manifest is rewritten after copies finish (synccli.py:85-86). If a file changes *while* being copied (or between scan and copy), the new state is recorded as synced but the copied bytes are stale — silently lost until the *next* mtime change. With edits "a few times per minute" this will happen in week one.

4. **mtime+size is weaker than claimed.** `st.st_mtime` is a float with filesystem-dependent granularity (1s on many filesystems); same-size saves within the same tick are invisible. If you keep mtime+size, capture sub-second fields and handle the copy/edit race (re-stat after copy).

**Under real load**

5. **The poll is not "cheap."** 50 000 files × 2/sec = 100k `stat` calls/sec plus a full diff, plus a full JSON manifest rewrite (50k entries, `indent=2` → multi-MB file, twice a second: constant disk churn, laptop battery/SSD wear). Either budget 1-5s scans, or make the manifest compact/no-indent, or scan incrementally (dir-level mtime as a pre-filter).

6. **"A long copy delays the next poll, which is fine" — it isn't.** One big file (a 2 GB build artifact) blocks detection of *all* other changes for its duration; a git checkout burst of thousands of files serializes into a minutes-long backlog. You need: copy size/time budget per cycle, or prioritized ordering, and never copy files you can't identify as stable.

**Decisions to change before writing code**

7. **"Reusing `sync()` directly" doesn't match the design.** `sync()` swallows copy errors silently (synccli.py:68-69) yet still records the file as synced in the manifest — failed copies are never retried, contradicting "log and continue" (you must *not* mark the entry fresh). It also has no delete or failure-path support. Plan a daemon-side sync loop, not reuse.

8. **Errors "log to stdout"** — a daemon should log to a file with rotation and a backoff on repeated failure of the same file (a permanently broken file currently gets re-read every poll, forever).

9. Also fix the latent bugs before building on this code: `collect_stale` skips `items[0]` (synccli.py:55), `filter_excluded` uses a mutable default arg and only matches top-level dirs, and `merge_manifest` is dead code.

Keep: mtime+size over hashing, and even the fixed 500 ms interval is defensible — but make scan cost and delete propagation the two design sections that currently don't exist.
run dir: /var/folders/k0/m1pgz3y90k34xd8h1b4tdkqw0000gp/T//synccli-eval-34634 (cost/tokens: sqlite3 ~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db, session title above)
[0m✓ [0mCritique DESIGN.md approach[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
### 1. Major Risks

- **Torn / partial copies & corrupted destination:** Copying immediately on change (`fsrc.read()`) catches files mid-write (compilers, downloads, non-atomic saves) and writes directly to `dst` (`open(dst, "wb")`) without temp-file + `os.replace`. Also, `fsrc.read()` loads the entire file into RAM, which will OOM on large files.
- **Corrupted manifest & false-sync state:** Rewriting the manifest in-place without atomic rename (`tmp` + `os.replace`) means any kill/crash mid-write leaves a truncated JSON file (`json.load` crashes on restart). Worse, `synccli.py:68` swallows `OSError` in `copy_file` and still writes `src_manifest` to `dst`, permanently marking failed copies as "synced".
- **Silent data divergence (no deletions or renames):** `changed_files()` (`synccli.py:36`) only checks `for rel in new_manifest`. Deleted files in `src` are never removed from `dst`, and renames copy the file anew while leaving the old path behind.

### 2. Wrong Assumptions

- **"Polling is cheap" (500 ms full scan of 50k files):** 50,000 `os.stat()` calls + sorting + dict diffing in Python takes hundreds of milliseconds to >1s depending on filesystem/OS cache. At a 500 ms interval, the daemon will run back-to-back at near 100% CPU/IOPS even when 0 files change.
- **"The manifest is small and we rewrite it in place on every poll cycle":** 50k entries in indented JSON is ~4–5 MB. Rewriting it every 500 ms (even when nothing changed) writes **~8–10 MB/s (~700 GB/day)** to disk for zero reason.
- **"mtime always changes on edit":** False for `git checkout`/`tar`/`rsync -a` (restores mtimes), intentional `touch`, or rapid same-size writes within coarse filesystem timestamp granularity.
- **"SQLite is another dependency":** `sqlite3` is in the Python standard library.
- **"Reusing `sync()` directly":** `sync()` re-reads and re-writes the full manifest on disk every call instead of keeping state in memory and persisting only when changes occur.

### 3. What Will Break Under Real Load (~50k files)

- **Idle workstation:** High constant CPU usage, laptop battery drain, and SSD wear from 100k `stat` syscalls/sec + 700 GB/day of no-op JSON writes.
- **Burst workloads (`git checkout`, `npm install`, build artifacts):**
  - Single-threaded blocking copies stall detection while copying thousands of files one by one.
  - Because `sync()` only writes the manifest *after* the entire batch finishes (`synccli.py:85`), an interruption mid-batch forces re-copying the entire batch.
  - No lockfile prevents concurrent `syncd` / `synccli` runs from clobbering `dst` and `.sync_manifest.json`.
  - Unreadable/locked files will retry and spam stdout twice per second forever without backoff.

### 4. Decisions to Change First

1. **Replace 500 ms full-tree polling with OS file events + slow reconciliation:** Use `watchdog` (FSEvents/inotify) with a 200–500 ms debounce/settle window, plus a slow full-tree reconciliation sweep (e.g., every 5–15 minutes). If polling only, keep manifest in memory, make interval configurable (e.g., 5–30s default), and only persist state when dirty.
2. **Make all writes atomic and streamed:** Copy in chunks (`shutil.copyfileobj`) to a temp file in `dst` and `os.replace()` on success. Write manifest updates via temp file + `os.replace()` (or stdlib `sqlite3` for O(1) incremental updates).
3. **Only record successful copies in state:** Never advance a file's manifest entry if `copy_file` failed; track retry backoff for failing files.
4. **Define deletion & exclusion semantics before coding:** Handle files present in `old_manifest` but missing from `src_manifest`, and exclude temp/VCS directories (`.git`, swap files) before scanning.
run dir: /var/folders/k0/m1pgz3y90k34xd8h1b4tdkqw0000gp/T//synccli-eval-34825 (cost/tokens: sqlite3 ~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db, session title above)
[0m✓ [0mCritique synccli DESIGN.md[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
**Verdict: don't build this as written.** Key findings (grounded in the actual `sync()` code):

**Critical**
1. **Failed copies recorded as success** — `sync()` writes the *source* manifest wholesale after copying, and `copy_file` swallows OSError. A transient error = file silently stale forever, never retried.
2. **No deletion/rename propagation** — `dst` becomes a growing superset of `src`, not a mirror. Editor temp files (`foo~`, `.swp`) orphan in `dst` permanently.

**High**
3. **"Manifest is small, atomicity not needed" is wrong twice**: 50k entries ≈ 5–7 MB JSON; rewritten non-atomically at 2 Hz ≈ 10–15 MB/s (~1 TB/day). Crash mid-rewrite = truncated JSON = daemon dead or re-copies 50k files.
4. **"Polling is cheap" is wrong**: ~50k stats × 2/s = ~100k syscalls/s, 0.1–0.3 s CPU per scan — can't meet its own 500 ms budget; continuous core burn.
5. **Non-atomic copy**: `dst` opened with `"wb"` (truncate), whole file `read()` into RAM — crash leaves torn file (then recorded as synced, per #1); 2 GB file = 2 GB RSS.

**Wrong assumptions called out**
- "mtime always changes on edit" — false: `cp -p`/tar/rsync preserve it; 1-s granularity misses same-second same-size edits.
- "SQLite = another dependency" — false: `sqlite3` is stdlib, and GOAL.md says stdlib-only.
- "reuse `sync()` directly" — it's a batch primitive (records source state, reads whole files); not a daemon cycle.

**Fix before writing code (in order)**
1. Manifest records *synced* state, updated per-file after copy lands — never from the source scan.
2. Propagate deletions/renames; decide trash-vs-delete policy.
3. SQLite (WAL) or temp+fsync+`os.replace`, written only when dirty.
4. Chunked copy to temp file in dst dir + `os.replace`.
5. Replace 2 Hz full rescan with FSEvents/inotify + a 30–60 s reconcile poll; hash only on detected change.

Also: make the interval configurable (hardcoding is the misuse), define dst-edit semantics, don't silently skip all dot-directories (`scan_files` drops `.config`, `.ssh`).

  - `copied`: the same ordered relative-path list currently returned by `sync()`; with dry-run, planned copies.
  - `skipped`: the number of source files unchanged according to the existing manifest comparison.
  - `src` and `dst`: the parsed source and destination argument strings.

  - Preserve existing exit codes and error behavior; resolve the treatment of help and parsing errors explicitly.

# Execution Plan: `--json` flag + `--dry-run` interplay for synccli

**Source of truth:** GOAL.md. **Current state:** `sync()` (synccli.py:72) returns the copied list; `main()` (synccli.py:90) prints one path per line. No JSON output exists. `incremental.py` and tests also live in the repo — the constraint says `sync()` has internal callers, so its signature is frozen.

---

## Task 0 — Explore: map callers and stdout writers
- **Agent:** `explore` (read-only)
- **Objective:** Enumerate every caller of `synccli.sync()` (esp. `incremental.py`) and every site that writes to stdout, to confirm what the frozen signature constrains and where output modes must be segregated.
- **Files:** none modified (research only).
- **Risks:** none (read-only).
- **Acceptance criterion:** A written list of `sync()` call sites + stdout-producing statements; confirms whether `incremental.py` imports `sync` or `main`.

## Task 1 — Design decision: where JSON is built, and what "skipped" means
- **Agent:** `cheap-design-review`
- **Objective:** Settle the two real design questions before code exists:
  1. Keep `sync()` returning `list[str]` (GOAL forbids signature change) → JSON object must be assembled in `main()`. Confirm.
  2. Define `skipped`: proposed = `(total files scanned in src) - len(copied)`, computed in `main()` via `scan_files(args.src)`. `src`/`dst` = the raw CLI argument strings.
  3. Error/empty behavior: `copied: []`, exit code still 0; argparse errors still go to stderr (stdout purity preserved).
- **Files:** none modified; output is a short decision note.
- **Risks:** `skipped` semantics are ambiguous in GOAL.md — wrong choice fails acceptance tests later; overloading `sync()` with a result object would violate the constraint.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Documented decision: JSON built in `main()` only; `skipped` formula agreed; `sync()` untouched.

## Task 2 — Implementation
- **Agent:** `cheap-code`
- **Objective:** Add `--json` flag and JSON output mode; tests.
- **Files to modify:**
  - `synccli.py`: add `parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")`; in `main()`, when `args.json`, emit exactly one `json.dumps` of `{"copied": copied, "skipped": N, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst}` to stdout and nothing else; human path-per-line loop unchanged otherwise. No new imports beyond stdlib (`json` already imported).
  - `test_synccli.py`: add tests — (a) `main(["src","dst","--json"])` captures stdout, parses as valid JSON, asserts keys `copied/skipped/src/dst`; (b) combined `--json --dry-run`: `copied` equals would-be-copied list and **no manifest is written** in dst; (c) second run with `--json` → `copied == []`, `skipped == 1`; (d) human mode regression: without `--json`, output is one path per line.
- **Risks:**
  - Stray `print` polluting stdout in JSON mode (e.g., a future warning) — tests must assert full stdout parses as a single JSON object.
  - mtime flakiness: `test_second_sync_is_noop`-style tests can be timing-sensitive; use explicit `os.utime` or content changes, not sub-second writes.
  - Path separators in `copied` are OS-dependent (`os.path.join`) — tests should compare against `os.path.join`-built expectations.
  - Capturing stdout: use `contextlib.redirect_stdout(io.StringIO())` (stdlib).
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes, including the new `--json` / `--dry-run` / human-mode tests; `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` prints exactly one JSON object; `python3 -m synccli --help` shows the new flag.

## Task 3 — Review
- **Agent:** `cheap-review` (mandatory, no exceptions)
- **Objective:** Independent review of the diff: stdout purity, frozen `sync()` signature, dry-run semantics, stdlib-only, test quality.
- **Files:** none modified (review only; findings routed back to Task 2's session).
- **Risks:** rubber-stamping a diff that quietly changes `sync()`'s return type or prints diagnostics in JSON mode.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Review verdict recorded; zero major findings (or majors fixed + re-reviewed before Task 4).

## Task 4 — Final verification
- **Agent:** main orchestrator (cheap; no new code)
- **Objective:** Run `python3 -m unittest -v` and a manual smoke: fresh dst → `--json` shows all files in `copied`; rerun → `copied: []`; `--json --dry-run` on fresh dst → `copied` lists files, dst stays empty, no `.sync_manifest.json` created; human mode output byte-identical to pre-change behavior.
- **Files:** none modified.
- **Risks:** environment (Python 3.10 vs newer local interpreter) — verify with `python3 --version`.
- **Acceptance criterion:** All tests green + smoke matrix passes; constraint checklist confirmed: no new deps, stdlib only, `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature unchanged.

diff --git a/incremental.py b/incremental.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..8c1c17d
--- /dev/null
+++ b/incremental.py
@@ -0,0 +1,40 @@
+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged
[0m

[0m• [0mFix stale iteration bug[90m Cheap-Mech Agent[0m
**Real defects in `incremental.py` (commit 41f2f42):**

1. **Major — `filter_excluded` shares state across calls** (`incremental.py:9-14`). The `excluded=[]` mutable default is created once and reused, and results are also appended into any caller-supplied list. A second call without the argument returns the union of *all previous calls'* results, so paths already seen get wrongly reported as excluded (and a caller's own list is mutated as a side effect). In a sync tool, wrongly excluded paths = silently skipped files.

2. **Minor — `copy_many` swallows every exception** (`incremental.py:17-27`). Bare `except Exception: continue` discards even non-I/O bugs (TypeError, etc.); the only signal is absence from the returned list, which fire-and-forget callers won't check. Combined with a manifest written from source state (the `sync()` pattern), a failed copy is recorded as synced and never retried.

3. **Minor — `merge_manifest` never drops deleted files and resolves conflicts by mtime only** (`incremental.py:30-40`). Entries for files removed from the source persist forever, and an entry is only replaced when `mtime` increases — a content change with an unchanged/restored mtime (e.g. `touch -r`, backup restores) keeps the stale state. The destination manifest drifts from reality.

4. **Minor — misleading contract in `merge_manifest`** (`incremental.py:30-33`). The name/docstring ("merge … **into the destination manifest**", parameter `dst_manifest_path`) implies it writes the file; it only reads it and returns a dict, leaving persistence to the caller.

## Task 1 — Design: JSON output contract
- **Objective:** Decide the exact semantics before code: definition of `skipped` (unchanged files = `len(src_manifest) − len(to_copy)`), JSON printed **only** on stdout (errors → stderr), `copied` = would-be list under `--dry-run`, indent/compact choice, `sync()` signature stays `(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)`.
- **Files:** none created; output is a short design note (chat or `DESIGN.md` appendix — flag for user approval since it's a repo file).
- **Agent:** `cheap-design` → `cheap-design-review` (contract/UX stakes: stdout purity, exit codes).
- **Risks:** Ambiguity in GOAL.md on whether `skipped` counts files or manifest entries; overloading `sync()`'s return (list vs dict) would break the "signature stable" constraint — resolve by keeping `sync()` returning the list and computing `skipped` in `main()`.
- **Acceptance:** Written spec answers: skipped definition, error channel, `copied` ordering (sorted, same as `scan_files`), exit-code table, and confirms `sync()` unchanged.

## Task 2 — Implement `--json` in the CLI layer
- **Objective:** Add `--json` flag; in that mode print exactly one JSON object `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}` to stdout, nothing else; human mode byte-identical to today; exit code unchanged (0).
- **Files:** `synccli.py` only (`main()`: add `parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")`, branch the output formatting; helper `_emit_json(...)` optional). No new imports beyond stdlib already present.
- **Agent:** `cheap-code` → `cheap-review` (mandatory).
- **Risks:** Any stray `print`/warning reaching stdout in JSON mode (e.g. argparse errors already go to stderr — verify); `src`/`dst` values: use `args.src`/`args.dst` as given, not normalized paths; ensure `skipped` is an int, not a list.
- **Acceptance (manual, scripted):**
  - `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` → stdout parses with `json.loads`, has all 4 keys, `copied` is a list of relative paths, `skipped` is int; a second identical run yields `copied: []`, `skipped` = file count.
  - `python3 -m synccli src dst --json --dry-run` → `copied` lists all files, dst directory untouched (no manifest written, no files created).
  - stdout of human mode unchanged vs current behavior (`diff` of outputs pre/post change).

## Task 3 — Unit tests for the JSON contract
- **Objective:** Lock the contract in `test_synccli.py`: valid JSON, all keys present with correct types, dry-run semantics (`copied` = would-be list, nothing written to dst), human mode unchanged, `--json` + `--dry-run` combined.

# Execution Plan: `--json` flag + `--dry-run` interplay for synccli

**Repo state observed:** `synccli.py` (CLI + `sync()` core), `incremental.py` (imports `copy_file` from synccli — the "internal caller"), `test_synccli.py`. `sync()` returns `to_copy` (list of relpaths) and writes the manifest; `main()` prints one path per line. There is currently **no notion of "skipped"** anywhere — this is the key design gap.

---

## Task 0 — Explore: pin down the contract before touching code

- **Objective:** Establish (a) all internal callers of `sync()` and its return-type consumers, (b) current stdout behavior in human/dry-run modes, (c) existing test patterns/fixtures in `test_synccli.py`, (d) decide what `skipped` means (recommendation: `len(scan_files(src)) - len(copied)`), and how to expose it without changing `sync()`'s signature (recommendation: a new `sync_report(src, dst, dry_run=False) -> dict` that `sync()` delegates to, so the stable signature stays).
- **Files:** none modified (read-only: `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `DESIGN.md`).
- **Pipeline agent:** `explore`
- **Risks:** Hidden callers of `sync()` outside the repo checkout; `skipped` semantics ambiguity (missing-in-dst vs. unreadable-source — note `copy_file` silently swallows `OSError` at synccli.py:69, which conflates "copied" with "skipped").
- **Acceptance criterion:** Written summary naming every `sync()` call site, a chosen `skipped` definition, and a chosen shape-preserving strategy (new function vs. keyword-only arg), confirmed against the `sync()` signature-stability constraint in GOAL.md.

## Task 1 — Design review (short, targeted)

- **Objective:** Approve the report-shape decision: `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}` — field types (list of relpaths, int, str paths as given on argv), ordering of keys, and how `--dry-run` alone behaves (human mode unchanged, prints "would copy" list exactly as today — no behavior change).
- **Files:** none modified; output is a short design note appended to the task description only.
- **Pipeline agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** Over-engineering (this is a ~30-line feature); review should be scoped to the contract, not architecture.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Signed-off note stating: skipped definition, exact JSON key order/types, and confirmation that human-mode output is byte-identical to today.

## Task 2 — Impl: core report + JSON plumbing

- **Objective:** Add `sync_report()` returning `{"copied", "skipped", "src", "dst"}`; refactor `sync()` to delegate (same list return, signature untouched). Extend `main()` with `--json` flag: when set, print exactly one `json.dumps(...)` to stdout and nothing else; exit code stays `0`. Works combined with `--dry-run` (`copied` = would-be-copied list).
- **Files:** modify `synccli.py` only.
- **Pipeline agent:** `cheap-code` (impl)
- **Risks:** (1) Accidentally changing `sync()`'s return type — must stay a list. (2) stray `print` leaking into JSON stdout (argparse help/errors go to stderr, which is fine). (3) `skipped` computed wrong under dry-run (dry-run must not mutate dst manifest — current code already returns early at synccli.py:82, keep that). (4) Latent bug noticed en route: `collect_stale()` (synccli.py:56) starts at index 1 and never reports the first item — out of scope, do not fix here, but flag it in the PR description.
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` prints a single parseable JSON object and nothing else on stdout; `--json --dry-run` lists would-be files in `copied`; human mode output identical to pre-change.

## Task 3 — Impl: tests

- **Objective:** Add unit tests to `test_synccli.py`: (1) `--json` output is valid JSON with exactly the four keys; `copied` is a list, `skipped` an int; (2) dry-run + JSON semantics: `copied` lists would-be files, dst dir and manifest unmodified; (3) human mode unchanged (one path per line, no JSON); (4) `sync()` still returns a list (signature-stability guard test).
- **Files:** modify `test_synccli.py` only.
- **Pipeline agent:** `cheap-code` (same instance as Task 2 if available via `task_id`; fresh otherwise)
- **Risks:** Flaky mtime-based tests (manifest comparison uses float mtime); tests must use tempdirs and isolate cwd/stdout capture.
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes with the new tests; new tests fail against the pre-change `synccli.py` (proves they test the feature).

## Task 4 — Review + final verification

- **Objective:** Independent review of the full diff: signature stability, stdout purity in JSON mode, no new dependencies (stdlib only, Python 3.10), no regression in human mode, error-path behavior (`copy_file` OSError swallow vs. skipped count) consistent.
- **Files:** none modified by reviewer; fixes routed back to Task 2/3 instance for minor findings.
- **Pipeline agent:** `cheap-review` (mandatory); escalate to `strong-review` only on major findings.
- **Risks:** Reviewer scope creep into the pre-existing `collect_stale` bug — keep it logged as follow-up, not fixed in this release.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Review verdict clean; full suite `python3 -m unittest -v` green; manual smoke: `python3 -m synccli --help` shows both flags; README untouched (or a one-line flag mention only if reviewer requires it).

- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** A documented design specifying CLI parsing, output structure, and calculation of `skipped` while preserving `sync()` stability.

---

### Task 2: Implement `--json` Flag and Output Formatting
- **Objective:** Add the `--json` option to `argparse` in `synccli.py`. When enabled, serialize and print exactly one JSON object with keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst` to `stdout`. Ensure `--dry-run` populates `copied` without copying files. Retain default newline-delimited human output when `--json` is omitted. Keep `sync()` signature and return value unchanged.
- **Files to create/modify:** `synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:**
  - Extra output written to `stdout` in JSON mode, violating the single JSON object constraint.
  - Incompatible CLI flag combinations or incorrect exit codes.

- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:**

  - With both `--dry-run` and `--json`, `copied` lists planned copies while target directory remains unchanged.
  - Running without `--json` produces identical line-by-line output as before.

- **Files to create/modify:** `test_synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:**
  - Inadequate isolation of `stdout` during test execution leading to test flakiness.
  - Missing edge cases such as empty directories or non-existent destination manifests.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` runs all existing and new tests, passing with 0 failures and 0 errors.

---

### Task 4: Implementation and Standards Code Review

- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only review).
- **Pipeline agent:** `review`
- **Risks:**
  - Hidden stdout writes or unhandled exceptions escaping JSON formatting.
  - Undetected regressions in human-mode CLI formatting.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Review approval confirming that all constraints from `GOAL.md` are satisfied and all unit tests pass.

## Execution plan

The release scope from `GOAL.md` is `--json` output and its interaction with `--dry-run`, with unchanged human output and a stable `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature. `README.md` specifies `python3 -m unittest -v` as the test command.

Pipeline roles: **explore** → `explore`; **design-review** → `cheap-design-review`; **impl** → `cheap-code`; **review** → `cheap-review`. Tasks 3–5 form one implementation batch, followed by mandatory review.

### 1. Establish the baseline and compatibility requirements
**Agent:** explore  
**Objective:** Confirm current CLI behavior, internal callers of `sync()`, and baseline test results.

**Files:** Read `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`; create/modify none.

**Risks:** Existing failures could be mistaken for regressions. In particular, `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, which appears inconsistent with existing tests.

**Acceptance criterion:** Report:
- Results of `python3 -m unittest -v`, identifying pre-existing failures.
- Current stdout and return-code behavior for normal and dry-run invocations.
- All discovered `sync()` callers and the compatibility constraints they impose.

### 2. Review the JSON contract and minimal implementation approach
**Agent:** design-review  
**Objective:** Resolve unspecified output details before implementation and approve an approach that preserves `sync()` compatibility.

**Files:** Read `GOAL.md`, `synccli.py`, and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none.

**Contract to review:**
- One JSON object with `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`.
- `copied` retains the existing relative-path list and ordering.
- Proposed `skipped`: source files unchanged according to the existing manifest comparison.
- Proposed `src` and `dst`: the supplied CLI argument strings.
- Dry-run reports planned copies without writing destination files or the manifest.
- Human mode and existing exit/error behavior remain intact.

**Risks:** `skipped` is undefined in the goal; copy errors are currently swallowed, so “unchanged” and “failed to copy” must not be conflated. Independently rescanning for statistics could produce inconsistent counts.

**Acceptance criterion:** A written review approves or corrects these definitions and identifies how copy paths and skipped counts will derive from the same synchronization decision without changing `sync()`’s signature or list return value.

### 3. Implement JSON output
**Agent:** impl  
**Objective:** Add `--json` and emit the approved payload while preserving existing behavior.

**Files:** Modify `synccli.py`; create none.

**Risks:** Extra stdout text would break machine parsing; refactoring could alter internal callers, dry-run side effects, or human output.

**Acceptance criterion:**
- `python3 -m synccli SRC DST --json` emits exactly one parseable JSON object with the approved fields and types.
- Adding `--dry-run` reports planned copies without filesystem writes.
- Without `--json`, stdout remains one relative path per line.
- `sync()` retains its existing signature and list return value.
- Imports and implementation remain Python 3.10 standard-library only.

### 4. Add meaningful CLI regression tests
**Agent:** impl  
**Objective:** Verify the output contract and dry-run behavior through `main()` using temporary directories and captured stdout.

**Files:** Modify `test_synccli.py`; create none.

**Risks:** Mock-only tests could miss actual copy/manifest side effects. Filesystem timestamps can make change tests flaky.

**Acceptance criterion:** Tests cover:
- Parsing the **entire** stdout as one JSON object; required keys and value types.
- First sync: expected copied paths, skipped count, destination contents, and manifest.
- Unchanged second sync: empty `copied` and the expected skipped count.
- Mixed changed/unchanged inputs with deterministic file-state changes.
- JSON dry-run: expected planned paths and no destination or manifest changes.
- Human-mode output and successful return code remaining unchanged.

All new tests pass; existing results match or improve upon the recorded baseline.

### 5. Document usage and output semantics
**Agent:** impl  
**Objective:** Explain how users consume JSON output and preview copies.

**Files:** Modify `README.md`; create none.

**Risks:** Documentation could overstate successful copying or leave `skipped` ambiguous.

**Acceptance criterion:** README includes:
- Normal JSON and combined JSON/dry-run command examples.
- An example payload and definitions of all four fields.
- Explicit dry-run no-write behavior.
- The existing usage/help and test commands.

### 6. Review the complete implementation and verify release readiness
**Agent:** review  
**Objective:** Independently check the implementation, tests, and documentation against the goal and approved contract.

**Files:** Read the diff for `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`; create/modify none during review.

**Risks:** Tests may pass while missing stdout contamination, API compatibility changes, or redundant synchronization scans.

**Acceptance criterion:**
- Run `python3 -m unittest -v` and compare results with the baseline.
- Verify CLI help exposes both flags and exercise the module entry point.
- Confirm stable `sync()` signature/return behavior, standard-library-only dependencies, exact JSON stdout, and dry-run no-write behavior.
- No unresolved review findings; minor fixes return to the same impl agent and receive re-review. Major findings trigger implementation escalation.

+++ b/incremental.py
@@ -0,0 +1,40 @@
+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged

# Execution Plan: `--json` output mode + `--dry-run` interplay

Repo state reviewed: `synccli.py` (103 lines), `test_synccli.py`, `DESIGN.md`, `README.md`. No files modified — plan only.

---

## Task 0 — Pre-flight exploration & design decision (skipped-count semantics)

- **Objective**: Read `DESIGN.md` and settle the one ambiguous design point: the definition of `skipped` (recommended: `len(scan_files(src)) - len(copied)` — files already in sync). Also decide where the JSON is assembled: `sync()` signature must stay stable (GOAL constraint), so compute `skipped` in `main()` via `scan_files()`, and build the payload there.
- **Files**: none modified (read-only); output = short decision note.
- **Agent**: `explore` (read `DESIGN.md`, confirm internal `sync()` callers), then `design-review` on the one-paragraph approach.
- **Risks**: wrong `skipped` semantics breaks acceptance tests later; hidden internal callers of `sync()` if signature gets touched.
- **Acceptance criterion**: written decision recorded in the task notes: skipped definition chosen, `sync()` untouched, JSON assembly location agreed.

## Task 1 — Implementation: `--json` flag in `main()`

- **Objective**: Add `--json` (`action="store_true"`) to the argparse parser. When set, print exactly one JSON object to stdout: `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}` where `copied` respects `--dry-run` (what WOULD be copied), `src`/`dst` are the CLI args verbatim. Human mode (one path per line) completely unchanged. Exit code stays `0`. No new imports beyond stdlib `json` (already imported).
- **Files**: modify `synccli.py` (only `main()`, ~10 lines).
- **Agent**: `cheap-code` (impl).
- **Risks**: stray `print` in JSON mode breaking stdout purity; accidentally reordering/human-printing in JSON mode; tempting refactor of `sync()` (forbidden — internal callers exist).
- **Acceptance criterion**: `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` emits a single parseable JSON object with all 4 keys, and nothing else on stdout; `--json --dry-run` lists would-be copies without touching `dst`; plain mode output byte-identical to before.

## Task 2 — Tests in `test_synccli.py`

- **Objective**: Add a `TestJsonOutput` class (stdlib `unittest`, run via `python3 -m unittest -v`): (a) valid JSON parseable with `json.loads`, (b) all four keys present with correct types (`copied` list, `skipped` int, `src`/`dst` str), (c) dry-run semantics — `copied` populated, dst dir NOT written (no manifest created), (d) `skipped == 0` on first sync and correct count on no-op second sync, (e) capture stdout via `contextlib.redirect_stdout` and assert no extra output.
- **Files**: modify `test_synccli.py` (append ~60 lines).
- **Agent**: `cheap-code` (impl; same instance as Task 1 via `task_id` reuse — small related change, context already warm).
- **Risks**: tests mutating shared tmp dirs; mtime granularity flakiness in no-op assertions; forgetting to exclude the manifest file from `skipped` count expectations.
- **Acceptance criterion**: `python3 -m unittest -v` passes with the new tests failing on pre-Task-1 code (red→green verified) and passing after.

## Task 3 — Review + final verification

- **Objective**: Independent review of the combined diff: GOAL constraint checklist (stdlib only, `sync()` signature unchanged, exit code unchanged, human mode untouched), stdout purity in JSON mode, test quality.
- **Files**: none (read-only); possible small fixes sent back to the Task 1/2 instance.
- **Agent**: `cheap-review` (mandatory even on short diff); escalate `strong-code` only on a major finding.
- **Risks**: review nitpicks scope-creeping into refactors — keep fixes minimal.
- **Acceptance criterion**: review returns no major findings; `python3 -m unittest -v` green; manual smoke `python3 -m synccli --help` shows both flags; README optionally gains one usage line (only if reviewer flags it).

# Execution Plan: JSON Output Mode & Dry-Run UX

Based on `GOAL.md` and `README.md`, here is the ordered task execution plan.

---

### Task 1: Codebase Audit & Contract Analysis
- **Objective:** Audit `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, and `test_synccli.py` to inspect the existing CLI flow and confirm all constraints: keeping `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature and return type unchanged, determining how `skipped` count is computed (total scanned source files minus copied files), and verifying Python 3.10 stdlib compatibility.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only audit).
- **Pipeline agent:** `explore`
- **Risks:** Altering `sync()` parameters or return value, breaking internal callers (such as continuous sync / `syncd` references in `DESIGN.md`).
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Documented contract confirming `sync()` remains `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False) -> list[str]`, with a clear formula for calculating `skipped` within the CLI layer using existing scan utilities (`scan_files`).

---

### Task 2: Architecture & JSON Interface Design Review
- **Objective:** Review the CLI argument handling and formatting architecture to ensure `--json` emits strictly a single JSON object to stdout (`{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}`), stdout remains clean of non-JSON logs, `--dry-run` behavior is preserved, human mode remains line-by-line, and process exit codes remain intact.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (review only).
- **Pipeline agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** Accidental stdout contamination (e.g., error messages or print statements leaking into stdout instead of stderr), schema divergence, or unexpected exit code behavior.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Approved design specification validating exact JSON schema compliance, stderr/stdout separation, and identical dry-run calculation between human and JSON modes.

---

### Task 3: Implement `--json` Flag and Output Formatting
- **Objective:** Add `--json` argument to `argparse` in `synccli.py`, compute `skipped` and metadata (`src`, `dst`), serialize the dictionary to stdout via `json.dumps()` when `--json` is supplied, ensure human mode remains unchanged when omitted, and keep `sync()` untouched or backwards-compatible.
- **Files to create/modify:** `synccli.py` (modify)
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Regressing default human-readable output formatting; leaking extra prints or newline characters to stdout in JSON mode; altering the public signature of `sync()`.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Invoking `synccli.main(["--json", src, dst])` outputs strictly valid JSON matching `{"copied": list, "skipped": int, "src": str, "dst": str}` to stdout; `--dry-run --json` outputs planned copies without modifying destination; omitting `--json` preserves line-by-line output.

---

### Task 4: Add Unit Tests for JSON Mode and Dry-Run Semantics
- **Objective:** Add automated test cases to `test_synccli.py` covering:
  1. Valid JSON format and key presence (`copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`).
  2. Correct `copied` listing and `skipped` count on initial sync.
  3. Correct `copied` and `skipped` on no-op secondary sync.
  4. Interplay of `--json` and `--dry-run` (verifying files are not written to destination and `copied` reflects pending work).
  5. Absence of extraneous characters or multiple JSON payloads on stdout.
- **Files to create/modify:** `test_synccli.py` (modify)
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Missing edge cases (e.g., empty source directory, nested subdirectories); test fixtures leaving dirty temporary files.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` executes all test suites with zero failures and covers all new JSON mode behaviors.

---

### Task 5: Final Code & Spec Conformance Review
- **Objective:** Perform a complete audit of modified files against all constraints in `GOAL.md`: zero external dependencies added, Python 3.10 stdlib compliance, stable `sync()` signature, pure stdout output, and comprehensive test coverage.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (review only).
- **Pipeline agent:** `review`
- **Risks:** Subtle stdlib incompatibilities or missed edge cases in error handling.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Verification pass confirming `git diff` shows only changes to `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`, `python3 -m unittest -v` exits with status 0, and no foreign dependencies exist.

## Execution plan

Based on `GOAL.md`, `README.md`, and the current implementation, this release adds JSON output to the existing one-shot CLI while preserving `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` and its list return value.

**Agent routing:** `explore` and `impl` use `github-copilot/gpt-6.1-sol medium`; `review` and `design-review` use `github-copilot/gpt-6.1-sol xhigh`.

### 1. Establish the compatibility baseline
**Agent:** `explore`

- **Objective:** Trace CLI output, manifest handling, dry-run behavior, and internal callers. Establish existing test results before implementation.
- **Files:** Read `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`. Create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Existing defects could be confused with feature regressions. In particular, `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, apparently contradicting `test_first_item_stale_is_included`.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Produce a baseline report containing:
  - Results of `python3 -m unittest -v`.
  - Current `sync()` signature and return contract.
  - Current CLI success/error behavior.
  - Any pre-existing failures, clearly identified.

### 2. Settle and review the output contract
**Agent:** `design-review`

- **Objective:** Approve a minimal implementation approach before coding.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none. Return the approved contract as task output.
- **Decisions to settle:**
  - `copied`: ordered relative paths returned by the existing synchronization logic; planned paths in dry-run mode.
  - `skipped`: number of source files considered unchanged by the existing manifest comparison.
  - `src` and `dst`: original CLI argument strings.
  - JSON mode emits one serialized object followed by an optional newline, with no per-path output.
  - Preserve current exit behavior; explicitly distinguish normal synchronization output from help, argument errors, and operational failures.
  - Obtain counts consistently without changing `sync()`’s signature or list return type. Prefer shared internal computation over independently scanning the source twice.
- **Risks:** Ambiguous meaning of `skipped`; inconsistent counts if the source changes between scans; accidental public API changes; expanding error-handling behavior beyond the release goal.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Review output specifies the exact field types, count computation, error-path expectations, and implementation strategy. All constraints in `GOAL.md` are accounted for.

### 3. Implement JSON output and regression coverage
**Agent:** `impl`

- **Objective:** Add `--json` and implement the approved contract using Python 3.10 standard-library facilities.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`. No new files expected.
- **Risks:** Human-readable lines leaking into JSON; dry-run writing files or manifests; changed ordering or return values; hidden coupling between CLI reporting and internal callers.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Add meaningful tests verifying:
  - Entire captured stdout parses with `json.loads()` as a single object.
  - All four keys exist with the approved types and values.
  - Initial synchronization reports copied paths and zero skipped files.
  - Unchanged synchronization reports `copied: []` and the correct skipped count.
  - A mixture of changed and unchanged files produces correct counts.
  - Empty input produces empty copied paths and zero skipped files.
  - `--json --dry-run` reports planned copies while leaving destination contents and manifest unchanged, including when the destination does not yet exist.
  - Human mode retains its exact one-path-per-line output, including dry-run behavior.
  - `sync()` retains its signature and list return contract.
  - Successful CLI return codes remain unchanged.

### 4. Independently review implementation and tests
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Audit correctness, compatibility, and test quality against the approved contract.
- **Files:** Inspect the diff in `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Tests may validate JSON parsing without checking values or filesystem effects; counting may diverge from synchronization decisions; feature work may conceal unrelated fixes.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Review confirms:
  - No extra stdout in normal JSON-mode execution.
  - Correct dry-run and skipped-count semantics.
  - Stable internal API and human output.
  - No new dependencies or Python features beyond 3.10.
  - No unresolved findings. Return fixes to `impl` and re-review the resulting diff.

### 5. Document the CLI contract

- **Objective:** Document the new option and its interaction with dry-run.

- **Acceptance criterion:** README includes runnable human, JSON, and JSON dry-run examples; defines all output fields; explains that dry-run reports intended copies without writing files. `python3 -m synccli --help` lists both flags clearly.

### 6. Perform the release verification gate

### Execution Plan: JSON Output Mode & Dry-Run UX

#### Task 1: Interface & Contract Specification
- **Objective**: Define the CLI contract for `--json`, specify path handling for `src`/`dst`, ensure `skipped` file calculation logic is exact, and guarantee `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature and return value remain 100% backward-compatible.
- **Files to create/modify**: None (design specification).
- **Pipeline Agent**: `design-review` (e.g., `cheap-design-review`)
- **Risks**: Modifying `sync()` signature or return type, which would break existing internal callers (`syncd`, programmatic tests); ambiguity on whether `skipped` count includes non-manifest or filtered files.
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion**: Written agreement confirming:
  1. `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature and return type (`list[str]`) are unchanged.
  2. `--json` output schema is strictly `{"copied": list[str], "skipped": int, "src": str, "dst": str}`.
  3. Strict stdout isolation: only the single JSON payload is emitted to stdout in `--json` mode.

---

#### Task 2: Implement `--json` Flag and Output Formatting
- **Objective**: Add `--json` argument to `argparse` in `synccli.py`, calculate `skipped` count (e.g., total scanned files minus files to copy), and format stdout as either the single JSON object (`--json`) or line-by-line paths (human mode). Support combined `--json --dry-run` behavior.
- **Files to create/modify**: `synccli.py`
- **Pipeline Agent**: `impl` (e.g., `cheap-code`)
- **Risks**: Leaking non-JSON output or logging to stdout; breaking default human-readable output formatting; miscalculating `skipped` when files change versus when files are untouched.
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion**:
  - `python3 -m synccli --json <src> <dst>` outputs parseable JSON containing keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`.
  - `python3 -m synccli --json --dry-run <src> <dst>` emits the JSON payload with proposed copies without mutating the destination directory.
  - Human mode (`python3 -m synccli <src> <dst>`) prints exactly one copied path per line as before.

---

#### Task 3: Unit Tests for JSON Output and Dry-Run Interplay
- **Objective**: Extend `test_synccli.py` with test cases verifying JSON output parsing, schema compliance, key presence, counts (`copied`, `skipped`), path values, `--dry-run` interaction, and human mode backward compatibility.
- **Files to create/modify**: `test_synccli.py`
- **Pipeline Agent**: `impl` (e.g., `cheap-code`)
- **Risks**: Flaky test harness when capturing stdout; incomplete coverage of edge cases (e.g., empty source directory, second-run with 0 copied and all skipped).
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion**:
  - `python3 -m unittest -v` passes cleanly.
  - Test suite verifies `json.loads(stdout)` succeeds and asserts keys `{"copied", "skipped", "src", "dst"}` across both fresh copy and no-op syncs with and without `--dry-run`.

---

#### Task 4: Code & Regression Review
- **Objective**: Verify implementation quality, adherence to Python 3.10 stdlib-only constraints, absence of new external dependencies, cleanliness of stdout stream, and stability of the `sync()` API contract.
- **Files to create/modify**: None (read-only audit).
- **Pipeline Agent**: `review` (e.g., `cheap-review`)
- **Risks**: Uncaught side-effects or stdout pollutants; regressions on existing `sync()` consumers.
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion**: Approval report confirming all requirements from `GOAL.md` are satisfied, diff touches only `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`, and test suite execution passes with zero failures.
