## Execution plan

The next release adds `--json` to the existing one-shot CLI, including dry-run reporting, while preserving human output, exit behavior, and `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)`.

Two points need resolution before implementation:
- `GOAL.md` does not define what counts as **`skipped`**.
- Inspection shows `collect_stale()` skips the first item, conflicting with an existing test. Establish the baseline before attributing failures to this feature.

### 1. Establish the baseline and map reporting data
**Agent:** `explore`

- **Objective:** Trace `main()` → `sync()`, identify existing callers and output paths, and determine how copied/skipped counts can be obtained consistently.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`. No modifications.
- **Risks:** `copy_file()` suppresses some errors, so the current returned list does not guarantee successful copies. A second source scan could yield inconsistent counts.
- **Acceptance criterion:** A short findings report identifies compatibility requirements, available reporting data, and the results of `python3 -m unittest -v`, including any pre-existing failures.

### 2. Review and settle the reporting contract
**Agent:** `design-review`

- **Objective:** Approve a minimal implementation approach before coding.
- **Files:** Inspect `GOAL.md` and `synccli.py`. No modifications required.
- **Decisions to settle:**
  - Recommended `skipped` meaning: source files unchanged according to the existing manifest comparison.
  - Recommended `src` and `dst` values: the supplied CLI argument strings.
  - Preserve both the signature and list-return behavior of `sync()`.
  - Derive reporting data from one synchronization snapshot, using a private helper if necessary.
  - Define how the single-object requirement applies to execution failures and argparse help/errors without changing exit behavior.
- **Risks:** Accidentally redefining copy success, expanding into error-handling changes, or adopting the separate daemon proposal in `DESIGN.md`.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Each decision has an explicit disposition; the approach preserves the existing public contract and uses only Python 3.10 standard-library facilities.

### 3. Implement JSON reporting and regression coverage
**Agent:** `impl`

- **Objective:** Add `--json`, implement the approved reporting contract, and cover it with meaningful tests.
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`.
- **Implementation requirements:**
  - Serialize one JSON object containing `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`.
  - Keep path-per-line output when `--json` is absent.
  - With `--dry-run --json`, report the paths that would be copied without modifying destination files or the manifest.
  - Preserve `sync()` compatibility.
- **Risks:** Extra stdout text, incorrect skipped counts, dry-run side effects, and changed return or exit behavior.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Tests verify:
  - The entire captured stdout parses as one JSON object.
  - Required keys, value types, and expected values.
  - Initial sync, unchanged repeat sync, and mixed changed/unchanged files.
  - JSON dry-run leaves destination content and manifest unchanged; a missing destination remains absent.
  - Human output, `sync()` signature/list return, and CLI exit codes remain compatible.
  - The full suite introduces no failures relative to the recorded baseline.

### 4. Review implementation and tests
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Independently check correctness and compatibility.
- **Files:** Review changes to `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`. No modifications.
- **Risks:** Tests may merely reproduce implementation assumptions; count calculation may diverge from the actual synchronization snapshot.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Review confirms the approved contract, stdout isolation, dry-run behavior, stable API, and dependency constraints. Any findings return to `impl` for correction and then receive another review.

### 5. Document the CLI contract
**Agent:** `impl`

- **Objective:** Document the new flag and its interaction with dry-run.
- **Files to modify:** `README.md`; adjust help text in `synccli.py` if necessary.
- **Risks:** Documentation could imply successful-copy guarantees that existing behavior does not provide or leave `skipped` ambiguous.
- **Acceptance criterion:** README includes working human, JSON, and JSON dry-run examples; explains all four fields and the approved `skipped` meaning; retains `python3 -m unittest -v` as the test command. `python3 -m synccli --help` describes both flags accurately.

### 6. Perform the final release review
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Verify the complete diff and documented behavior.
- **Files:** Review `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`. No modifications.
- **Risks:** Documentation-stage changes may introduce regressions; pre-existing failures may obscure release readiness.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Run `python3 -m unittest -v` and CLI smoke checks for human, JSON, and combined dry-run modes, preferably under Python 3.10. Confirm no new dependencies or unreviewed changes. Report any baseline failures explicitly; an all-green release gate requires their separate disposition.

# Execution Plan: `--json` output mode for synccli

## Key design facts (from reading the code)

- `sync()` (synccli.py:72) already takes `dry_run` and returns the copied list — **no signature change needed**. The JSON payload can be fully assembled in `main()`.
- `skipped` is derivable: `len(scan_files(src)) - len(copied)`.
- Human mode is just the `for rel in copied: print(rel)` loop at synccli.py:97-98 — JSON mode must bypass it and print exactly one `json.dumps` line.

---

## Task 1 — Explore: confirm constraints & edge cases
- **Objective:** Verify no other internal callers of `sync()`/`main()` exist; pin down `skipped` semantics (files considered but not copied; unreadable-source case in `copy_file` synccli.py:68 counts as "copied" today — decide if it stays that way).
- **Files:** none (read-only).
- **Agent:** `explore`
- **Risks:** hidden callers of `sync()` relying on the list return type; ambiguity in `skipped` definition.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Written summary stating all `sync()` call sites and a chosen definition of `skipped` consistent with GOAL.md.

## Task 2 — Design review of the output strategy
- **Objective:** Approve approach: payload assembled in `main()` only; `sync()` untouched; `--json` + `--dry-run` compose naturally because `sync(dry_run=True)` already returns the would-copy list.
- **Files:** none (text design note in response).
- **Agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** alternative (changing `sync()` to return a dict) rejected because GOAL.md mandates signature stability.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Design note with an explicit "approved" verdict and no open blocking questions.

## Task 3 — Implement `--json` flag
- **Objective:** Add `parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")`; in JSON mode, print exactly one object to stdout: `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}`, exit code 0 unchanged; human mode byte-identical to today.
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py` (only `main()`, ~lines 90-99).
- **Agent:** `cheap-code` (impl)
- **Risks:** accidentally printing human lines before the JSON; `"src"/"dst"` must echo the CLI args verbatim.
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` emits one parseable JSON line on stdout and nothing else; `--json --dry-run` lists would-be copies in `copied` and copies nothing to disk.

## Task 4 — Unit tests
- **Objective:** Add to `test_synccli.py`: (a) JSON output is valid JSON with exactly the 4 required keys; (b) dry-run semantics — `copied` non-empty, dst dir unchanged, no manifest written; (c) human mode unchanged (one path per line); (d) second `--json` run has `copied == []` and `skipped == N`.
- **Files to modify:** `test_synccli.py` (new test class, stdlib `unittest` + `contextlib.redirect_stdout` / `subprocess` pattern consistent with existing style).
- **Agent:** `cheap-code` (impl)
- **Risks:** capturing stdout must not swallow the JSON assertion; temp-dir reuse between tests.
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes, including ≥4 new tests, zero new dependencies.

## Task 5 — Code review (mandatory)
- **Objective:** Independent review of Tasks 3-4 diff: contract compliance (single JSON object, exit code unchanged, human mode untouched), no `sync()` signature change, test quality.
- **Files:** none (read-only).
- **Agent:** `cheap-review`
- **Risks:** reviewer missing the "nothing else on stdout" subtlety (e.g. argparse `--help`/errors go to stderr — fine; verify nothing prints to stdout on the success path).
- **Acceptance criterion:** Verdict with zero major findings; minor findings recycled to the same impl session.

## Task 6 — Final verification
- **Objective:** Run the full suite and the two CLI invocations from Task 3's criterion manually.
- **Files:** none.
- **Agent:** orchestrator (me) via `bash`
- **Risks:** none.
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` green; manual `--json` and `--json --dry-run` runs match GOAL.md exactly.

Ordering: 1 → 2 → 3 ∥ 4 → 5 → 6. No files modified in this response.

Based on `GOAL.md` and `README.md`, the next release adds JSON output while preserving human output, exit behavior, Python 3.10 stdlib-only compatibility, and `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)`.

**No modifications applied.** The tasks below are for subsequent execution.

### 1. Establish the baseline and map output behavior
**Agent:** `explore`

- **Objective:** Trace CLI parsing, synchronization, manifest handling, stdout/stderr behavior, and internal callers of `sync()`. Run the existing test suite and record baseline failures.
- **Files:** Read `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`; no source changes.
- **Risks:** Existing defects could be mistaken for regressions. In particular, inspection shows `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, conflicting with `test_first_item_stale_is_included`.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** An exploration report identifies the output integration point, existing signature and return contract, caller compatibility requirements, and results of `python3 -m unittest -v`, including any pre-existing failures.

### 2. Resolve the JSON contract and approve the approach
**Agent:** `design-review`

- **Objective:** Approve a minimal approach that keeps reporting at the CLI boundary and preserves `sync()` compatibility. Resolve details not specified by the goal:
  - Define `skipped`—proposed meaning: source files unchanged according to the manifest.
  - Define `src` and `dst`—proposed values: the supplied CLI arguments.
  - Confirm JSON reporting on successful invocations and existing behavior for help, argument errors, and synchronization failures.
  - Select how to obtain the skipped count without altering the public signature or introducing inconsistent duplicate scans.
- **Files:** Review `GOAL.md`, `synccli.py`, and `test_synccli.py`; no file changes required.
- **Risks:** `skipped` could misleadingly include failed copies; extra scanning could produce inconsistent counts; changing copy-error behavior could alter existing semantics.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** A reviewed contract specifies field types, counting rules, path representation, dry-run semantics, and error/output behavior, with an implementation approach that preserves the existing signature and list return value.

### 3. Implement JSON reporting
**Agent:** `impl`

- **Objective:** Add `--json` and emit one JSON object containing `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`. With `--dry-run`, report candidate paths without writing files or the manifest. Preserve the existing human-output branch.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py`; no new files expected.
- **Risks:** Human path printing could leak into JSON stdout; refactoring could change internal callers or cause dry-run writes; reporting might inadvertently change exit behavior.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:**
  - The complete stdout from a successful JSON invocation parses as exactly one JSON object.
  - Normal and dry-run JSON results follow the approved contract.
  - Human mode still emits one relative path per line.
  - `sync()` retains its signature and return contract.
  - No third-party dependencies are introduced.

### 4. Add regression coverage and usage documentation
**Agent:** `impl`

- **Objective:** Add meaningful CLI-level unit tests and document the new flags.
- **Files:** Modify `test_synccli.py` and `README.md`.
- **Risks:** Parsing only part of stdout could conceal extra output; dry-run tests could miss manifest changes; filesystem timestamps could make fixtures unreliable.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:**
  - Tests parse captured stdout in full and assert required keys, types, and expected values.
  - Cases cover an initial copy, unchanged files with a nonzero skipped count, a mixed changed/unchanged source, and `--json --dry-run`.
  - Dry-run tests verify destination contents and manifest state remain unchanged.
  - Regression tests verify exact human output and successful return codes.
  - README examples show JSON mode and combined dry-run usage, including the meaning of `skipped`.
  - `python3 -m unittest -v` shows no new failures relative to task 1.

### 5. Review the complete change and verify release readiness
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Independently audit implementation, tests, and documentation against the approved contract and `GOAL.md`.
- **Files:** Review changes to `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`; reviewer makes no edits.
- **Risks:** Tests may mirror implementation assumptions rather than the contract; baseline failures may mask regressions; unrelated daemon or incremental-helper changes could expand scope.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:**
  - Independent test execution confirms all new tests pass and no new failures exist.
  - Review confirms stdout purity, dry-run non-mutation, human-output compatibility, stable `sync()` API, unchanged exit behavior, and Python 3.10 stdlib-only usage.
  - All blocking findings are resolved by `impl` and re-reviewed.
  - Any baseline failure remains explicitly reported; the suite is described as fully passing only if it actually passes.

**Scope boundary:** The continuous-sync proposal in `DESIGN.md` and unrelated helper defects are separate work. A pre-existing failure requiring repair should receive its own explicitly scoped implementation-and-review task.

### Execution Plan: JSON Output Mode & Dry-Run UX

---

#### Task 1: Codebase & Interface Exploration
- **Objective:** Analyze existing CLI argument parsing in `main()`, helper functions for file scanning/manifest computation, and caller contracts for `sync()` to determine how `skipped` count is computed without altering `sync()` signature or return type.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only: `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, `README.md`).
- **Pipeline agent:** `explore`
- **Risks:** Misidentifying how `skipped` is defined (e.g., total scanned source files minus changed files to copy vs. unchanged destination files), or proposing signature changes to `sync()` that break existing internal callers.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Documented breakdown of `main()` execution flow confirming exact calculations for `copied`, `skipped` (`len(src_manifest) - len(to_copy)`), `src`, and `dst` without touching the `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature.

---

#### Task 2: Architecture & Design Review
- **Objective:** Review and approve the interface specification for `--json`, ensuring single JSON payload on stdout, strict stdout isolation (no warning/progress leaks), exit code preservation, and behavior under combinations of `--json` and `--dry-run`.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (design review).
- **Pipeline agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** Output pollution on stdout breaking automated JSON parsers, or introducing dependencies outside Python 3.10 standard library.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Approved design specifying `json.dumps({"copied": copied, "skipped": skipped, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst})` on stdout exclusively when `--json` is supplied, exit code remaining 0 on success, and human output formatting preserved when `--json` is omitted.

---

#### Task 3: Implementation of `--json` CLI Flag and Output Logic
- **Objective:** Add `--json` argument to `argparse` in `synccli.py`, extract manifest statistics to compute `skipped`, and format output as either human-readable lines (default) or a single JSON object (`--json`), supporting both live runs and `--dry-run`.
- **Files to create/modify:** `synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Inadvertent stdout writes (e.g. trailing prints, newline mismatches) or mutating `sync()` parameters/return values.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** 
  1. `synccli.py` accepts `--json` alongside existing arguments.
  2. Running `python3 -m synccli <src> <dst> --json` emits exclusively a valid JSON string matching `{"copied": [...], "skipped": <int>, "src": <str>, "dst": <str>}`.
  3. Combined `--dry-run --json` reports expected copy list without modifying files or manifest.
  4. Non-JSON mode output remains identical to prior behavior.

---

#### Task 4: Unit Test Implementation for JSON Mode and Interplay
- **Objective:** Add unit tests to `test_synccli.py` covering `--json` output format validation, key verification (`copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`), stdout cleanliness, dry-run interplay, and regression coverage for human mode.
- **Files to create/modify:** `test_synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Incomplete CLI runner test isolation or test stdout capture leaking into test runner outputs.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** New test cases in `test_synccli.py` run via `python3 -m unittest -v` pass, asserting:
  - Stdout parses successfully with `json.loads`.
  - All four required keys are present and correctly typed.
  - `skipped` correctly reflects unchanged files count on subsequent syncs.
  - `--dry-run` with `--json` populates `copied` without creating destination files or manifest.

---

#### Task 5: Final Review & Quality Audit
- **Objective:** Inspect changes across `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py` to ensure compliance with all constraints in `GOAL.md`: zero external dependencies, Python 3.10 stdlib compliance, stable `sync()` signature, and full test suite execution.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (code audit).
- **Pipeline agent:** `review`
- **Risks:** Unintended side effects or edge cases (e.g., empty source directories, non-existent paths, unhandled OS errors) leaking unformatted traces to stdout.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Code review pass verifying clean diffs, no newly introduced dependencies, stable `sync()` signature, and 100% pass rate on `python3 -m unittest -v`.

# Execution plan: `--json` flag + dry-run UX

**Key context driving the plan**
- `sync()` (synccli.py:72) must keep its exact signature `sync(src, dst, dry_run=False)` and its list return — tests and internal callers depend on it → all JSON work lives in `main()` (synccli.py:90).
- GOAL specifies a `"skipped": N` field, but nothing in the code today counts skips: `copy_file` (synccli.py:62) silently swallows `OSError`, so skip information is currently destroyed before it reaches any caller. This is the main design decision to settle first.
- DESIGN.md describes a future daemon that "logs to stdout" — directly conflicts with "nothing else on stdout in JSON mode"; constraint must be written down.
- Side finding (out of scope, report-only): `collect_stale` skips `items[0]` (synccli.py:55) and `filter_excluded` has a mutable default arg (incremental.py:10).

---

## Task 1 — Pin down `skipped` semantics and output architecture
- **Agent:** `design-review` (cheap-design-review)
- **Objective:** Decide, before any code: (a) what `skipped` counts — recommended: files in manifest but not copied (unchanged), computed as a derived value in `main()`, *not* a `sync()` change; (b) confirm JSON assembly goes in `main()` only, keeping `sync()` untouched.
- **Files:** none created; a short decision note in the task output (or appended to DESIGN.md — reviewer's choice, text only).
- **Risks:** Ambiguous spec → wrong field implemented; changing `sync()`'s return type breaks `test_synccli.py:44` and the "signature stable" constraint.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Written decision covering: meaning of `skipped` under normal sync, under `--dry-run` (recommended: count of files that would NOT be copied), and confirmation that `sync()` signature/return are untouched.

## Task 2 — Implement `--json` in `main()`
- **Agent:** `impl` (cheap-code)
- **Objective:** Add `--json` (`action="store_true"`) to the argparse parser; when set, build `{"copied": copied, "skipped": N, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst}` and `print(json.dumps(payload))` exactly once; human path-per-line loop unchanged otherwise; still `return 0`.
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py` (only `main()`, ~lines 90–99). No changes to `sync()`, `scan_files`, `copy_file`, or `incremental.py`. No new imports beyond stdlib already present (`json` is already imported).
- **Risks:** Accidentally printing anything else to stdout in JSON mode; tempting refactor of `sync()` to return skip counts (forbidden); `json.dumps` of non-serializable values (paths are plain `str`, safe).
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m synccli SRC DST --json` emits exactly one line that `json.loads()` parses to an object with all four keys; `python3 -m synccli SRC DST` output is byte-identical to before; exit code 0 in both modes; `--dry-run --json` lists would-be files in `copied`.

## Task 3 — Unit tests in `test_synccli.py`
- **Agent:** `impl` (cheap-code) — ideally the *same* instance resumed via `task_id` after Task 2
- **Objective:** New test class (e.g. `TestJsonOutput`) covering: (1) output is valid JSON with keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`; (2) `copied` matches the files created in setUp; (3) `--dry-run --json` reports would-be copies and leaves dst without the file or manifest; (4) stdout purity — exactly one line/one JSON object, nothing else; (5) human mode regression (one path per line).
- **Files to modify:** `test_synccli.py` (append class; reuse the `setUp` tmp-dir pattern from `TestSync`).
- **Risks:** Capturing stdout needs `contextlib.redirect_stdout` (stdlib, allowed); testing `main()` requires calling `synccli.main([...argv...])` directly rather than subprocess — keep it in-process like the rest of the file.
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes with zero failures including the new class; suite contains ≥1 test asserting dry-run + JSON combined semantics.

## Task 4 — Mandatory review of the diff
- **Agent:** `review` (cheap-review; if majors → escalate `strong-code`)
- **Objective:** Independent read-only review of Tasks 2–3 diff.
- **Files:** none modified by reviewer.
- **Risks it must check:** stdout purity regression; `sync()` signature drift; accidental new dependency or non-3.10 syntax; human-mode behavior change; `skipped` semantics matching the Task 1 decision; test asserting the *spec*, not the implementation.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Review delivered with findings classified minor/major; zero open majors.

## Task 5 — Final verification (orchestrator)
- **Agent:** orchestrator (or `strong-review` if a heavy audit is requested)
- **Objective:** Run `python3 -m unittest -v`; manually run both CLI modes on a temp fixture and diff human-mode output against `git stash` baseline.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Full suite green; human output identical to pre-change baseline; JSON output parses with `json.loads` and satisfies every bullet in GOAL.md.

---

**Sequencing:** 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 (strict order; 2 and 3 may share one cheap-code session). Nothing in the plan touches `incremental.py` or the DESIGN.md daemon — that work is explicitly out of scope for this release.

# Execution Plan: JSON Output Mode & Dry-Run UX

Based on `GOAL.md`, `README.md`, and the existing codebase, here is the ordered execution plan.

---

### Task 1: Codebase & Dependency Exploration
- **Objective:** Identify all call sites and usages of `synccli.sync()` (e.g., in `incremental.py`, tests, or potential daemon modules) to ensure backward compatibility constraints are mapped before changes begin.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only).
- **Pipeline agent:** `explore`
- **Risks:** Missing an internal caller that relies on the return value or argument structure of `sync()`.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Complete inventory of `sync()` references across the repository confirming how `sync()` is consumed and proving that helper refactoring will not break callers.

---

### Task 2: Architecture & Output Contract Design Review
- **Objective:** Finalize the design for JSON mode: define the exact JSON payload format (`{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}`), determine how `skipped` is calculated (`len(src_manifest) - len(to_copy)`), ensure stdout exclusivity (no debug prints or headers to stdout), and verify that `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature remains unchanged.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only review / architectural specification).
- **Pipeline agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** Ambiguity on `skipped` count calculation or unintentional signature drift on `sync()`.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** A finalized design specification confirming:
  1. `sync()` signature and return type (`list[str]`) remain unchanged.
  2. Calculation for `skipped` handles first run, unchanged files, and dry-run accurately.
  3. JSON output is emitted as a single valid JSON object on stdout with zero additional stdout emissions.

---

### Task 3: Core CLI Implementation (`--json` & `--dry-run` Interplay)
- **Objective:** Implement `--json` CLI argument in `synccli.py`. In JSON mode, compute the output object (`copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`) and output it via `json.dumps()` to stdout. When `--json` is absent, preserve standard line-by-line output. In `--dry-run --json` mode, populate `copied` with planned copies and compute `skipped` without altering destination files.
- **Files to create/modify:** `synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:**
  - Emitting diagnostic or trailing output to stdout in `--json` mode.
  - Modifying `sync()` signature or breaking standard human-readable stdout format.
  - Introducing third-party dependencies outside Python 3.10 stdlib.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:**
  - Running `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` produces exactly one valid JSON object with keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`.
  - Combining with `--dry-run` lists would-be copied files in `copied` while leaving destination unmodified.
  - Running without `--json` preserves identical human-readable output (one file path per line).
  - Exit code remains `0` on success.

---

### Task 4: Unit Test Suite Extension
- **Objective:** Add comprehensive unit and integration tests in `test_synccli.py` to validate `--json` output structure, schema compliance, stdout hygiene, and dry-run behavior.
- **Files to create/modify:** `test_synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Incomplete test coverage for CLI invocation paths or failure to verify that non-JSON stdout remains pristine.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:**
  - New test class covering:
    - CLI invocation with `--json`: verifies valid JSON string output containing all required keys.
    - Repeated sync scenario: verifies `skipped` count equals total files when no files changed.
    - `--json --dry-run` scenario: verifies `copied` reflects prospective copies and no files are written.
    - CLI without `--json`: verifies regression safety for line-by-line output.
  - `python3 -m unittest -v` exits with status 0 and all tests passing.

---

### Task 5: Quality & Safety Review

- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only audit).

# Execution Plan: `--json` output mode + `--dry-run` UX for synccli

Repo state read: `GOAL.md`, `README.md`, `DESIGN.md`, `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`. No files modified.

## Task 0 — Recon: output paths & callers of `sync()`
- **Objective:** Confirm every stdout write site and every internal caller of `sync()` so the "signature stays stable" constraint is honored.
- **Files:** none (read-only).
- **Agent:** `explore`.
- **Risks:** hidden callers of `sync()` (e.g. future `syncd` per DESIGN.md) depending on its return type (currently `list[str]`).
- **Acceptance:** written summary listing all call sites of `sync()` and all `print()`/stdout writes in `synccli.py` (they are confined to `main()`, synccli.py:97-98 — confirm nothing else).

## Task 1 — Design decision: semantics of `skipped` + where JSON is emitted
- **Objective:** Pin down (a) what `skipped: N` counts — files present in src but unchanged since last manifest (`len(src_manifest) − len(copied)` is the natural reading); (b) that JSON emission lives **only in `main()`**, keeping `sync()` returning `list[str]` (signature-stable constraint); (c) `--json --dry-run` → `copied` = would-be copies, no filesystem writes.
- **Files:** none (decision recorded in the task brief).
- **Agent:** `design-review`.
- **Risks:** ambiguous "skipped" definition (GOAL.md doesn't define it); emitting JSON inside `sync()` would leak formatting into library code.
- **Acceptance:** one-paragraph decision note answering both questions, no code.

## Task 2 — Implement `--json` in `main()`
- **Objective:** Add `parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")`; when set, print exactly one `json.dumps({"copied": copied, "skipped": N, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst})` to stdout, exit code still 0; human mode keeps one-path-per-line.
- **Files:** modify `synccli.py` (only `main()`, lines 90-99).
- **Agent:** `impl` (cheap-code).
- **Risks:** stray prints reaching stdout in JSON mode; accidentally changing `sync()`'s signature or return type; using a new dependency (forbidden — stdlib only, `json`/`argparse` already imported).
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` outputs one parseable JSON object with the 4 keys; `--json` alone unchanged exit code 0; `git diff` touches no other function.

## Execution plan

The next-release scope is `--json` plus its `--dry-run` behavior, as specified in `GOAL.md`. Keep Python 3.10 compatibility, use only the standard library, and preserve `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` and its list return value.

The repository also contains a separate v2 daemon proposal in `DESIGN.md`; it is outside this release’s scope.

**Suggested model allocation:** `medium` for `explore` and `impl`; `xhigh` for `design-review` and `review`, using the two supplied `github-copilot/gpt-6.1-sol` configurations.

### 1. Establish the baseline and compatibility surface
- **Agent:** `explore` — read-only.
- **Objective:** Run the existing tests; inspect CLI output, exit behavior, manifest handling, and internal `sync()` callers. Identify existing failures before implementation.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `README.md`, and `GOAL.md`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Existing defects may be mistaken for feature regressions. Static inspection already shows that `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, conflicting with `test_first_item_stale_is_included`.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Report the result of `python3 -m unittest -v`, exact failing tests, the current `sync()` contract, and the relevant CLI entry point. No working-tree changes.

### 2. Resolve the JSON contract before implementation
- **Agent:** `design-review` — read-only.
- **Objective:** Approve the smallest compatible implementation approach and resolve requirements left implicit in `GOAL.md`.
- **Files:** Inspect `GOAL.md`, `synccli.py`, and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none.
- **Contract to confirm:**
  - `copied` contains relative paths in existing order, including planned paths during dry-run.
  - `skipped` is an integer counting source entries unchanged according to the existing manifest comparison.
  - `src` and `dst` preserve the supplied argument strings.
  - Successful JSON invocations emit exactly one object, without path lines or other stdout text.
  - Existing error and exit behavior remains intact; clarify whether “single JSON object” also applies to failed invocations.
- **Risks:** `sync()` returns only copied paths; deriving `skipped` through another scan adds work and can observe a different filesystem snapshot. Also, existing copying can silently skip I/O failures, so the result currently does not guarantee successful copies.
- **Acceptance criterion:** A written decision covers these semantics and selects a way to obtain counts without changing the public signature, return type, or copy-error policy. Any unresolved requirement is explicitly identified.

### 3. Repair a confirmed baseline blocker
- **Agent:** `impl` — `cheap-code`; conditional on Task 1 confirming the failure.
- **Objective:** Make the minimal correction needed for the existing stale-entry test.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py`; modify `test_synccli.py` only if additional meaningful coverage is necessary.
- **Risks:** Expanding into unrelated cleanup in `incremental.py` or synchronization behavior.
- **Acceptance criterion:** The existing first-item stale test passes, and no new baseline failures appear. The diff is limited to the confirmed defect.

### 4. Review the baseline repair
- **Agent:** `review` — `cheap-review`; required if Task 3 executes.
- **Objective:** Independently verify the repair’s correctness and scope.
- **Files:** Inspect the Task 3 diff; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Masking a failure by weakening its test.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Reviewer approves the minimal repair and test evidence. Minor findings return to the same implementation agent; major findings trigger escalation.

### 5. Implement JSON output, regression tests, and usage documentation
- **Agent:** `impl` — `cheap-code`.
- **Objective:** Implement the approved contract and document its usage.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`. No new files or dependencies expected.
- **Risks:** Mixed human/JSON stdout, incorrect unchanged counts, dry-run filesystem writes, or breaking internal callers.
- **Acceptance criterion:**
  - Tests parse the **entire captured stdout** as one JSON object and assert the required keys, types, paths, and counts.
  - Cases cover initial copying, unchanged files, mixed changed/unchanged files, and an empty source.
  - Combined `--json --dry-run` reports planned copies while leaving destination files and manifest unchanged, including when the destination does not yet exist.
  - Human mode retains one relative path per line, including dry-run.
  - Existing direct `sync()` calls retain their signature and list results.
  - `python3 -m unittest -v` passes under Python 3.10.
  - `README.md` explains JSON fields and provides JSON and combined dry-run examples.

### 6. Review and verify the release change
- **Agent:** `review` — `cheap-review`, using `xhigh` for the final audit.
- **Objective:** Audit the complete feature diff against `GOAL.md` and independently verify observable CLI behavior.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Tests passing while overlooking output contamination, changed exit status, or repeated-scan inconsistencies.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Reviewer confirms the full suite passes, checks `python3 -m synccli --help`, exercises human/JSON/dry-run CLI modes, and verifies the dependency and API constraints. All findings are resolved before approval.

# Execution Plan: `--json` output mode + dry-run UX

## Task 0 — Baseline exploration (read-only)

- **Agent:** `explore`
- **Objective:** Establish the current state before touching anything. Two things I already spotted while reading:
  1. `collect_stale` (synccli.py:55) iterates `range(1, len(items))` — it never inspects the first item, yet `test_first_item_stale_is_included` (test_synccli.py:22) expects `"x.txt"` included. The suite is likely red today. Confirm.
  2. `copy_file` (synccli.py:62) swallows `OSError`, but `sync()` still returns the path in `copied` — so "copied" can be a lie. Relevant to JSON semantics.
- **Files:** none modified; report only.
- **Risks:** none (read-only).
- **Acceptance:** report states (a) actual result of `python3 -m unittest -v`, (b) list of internal callers of `sync()` (GOAL says they exist), (c) confirmation of the `copy_file` mismatch.

## Task 1 — Design decision: `skipped` semantics + where JSON is built

- **Agent:** `design-review`
- **Objective:** Decide, before code:
  1. What `skipped` means: `len(src_manifest) - len(copied)`? Does it count in dry-run (where nothing is copied — is everything "skipped" or is skipped = "would skip")?
  2. Where the JSON object is assembled. Constraint: `sync()` signature is frozen. Options: (a) `main()` recomputes skipped via `build_manifest(src)`, (b) new internal helper `sync_report(src, dst, dry_run)` returning `(copied, total)`, with `sync()` delegating to it — signature untouched.
  3. Confirm `sync()` keeps returning the plain list so internal callers are unaffected.
- **Files:** none modified; short decision note (in response, or DESIGN.md only if the team wants it persisted — flag for user).
- **Risks:** picking "skipped" semantics that break dry-run expectations in the GOAL; changing `sync()` return shape by accident.
- **Acceptance:** written decision covering both flags' interplay, with the chosen option justified against the frozen-signature constraint.

## Task 2 — Implementation: `--json` flag + JSON output

- **Agent:** `impl` (cheap-code)
- **Objective:** Add `--json` to the argparser (synccli.py:94). In `main()`, branch on the flag:
  - JSON mode: `print(json.dumps({"copied": copied, "skipped": N, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst}))` — exactly one line, nothing else on stdout. Exit code unchanged (0).
  - Human mode: existing loop untouched (GOAL §3).
- **Files:** modify `synccli.py` (main only, plus optional new internal helper per Task 1 decision). No new imports beyond stdlib already present (`json` is already imported).
- **Risks:** accidental extra stdout output (argparse errors go to stderr — verify); Python 3.10 stdlib only (trivially met); drifting from the Task 1 decision on `skipped`.
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` emits exactly one parseable JSON object with all four keys; piping stdout to `json.load` succeeds; `--dry-run --json` lists would-be files in `copied` and writes nothing to `dst`.

## Task 3 — Implementation: unit tests

- **Agent:** `impl` (cheap-code, same instance as Task 2 via `task_id` — small iteration, context reuse)
- **Objective:** Add to `test_synccli.py`:
  - JSON mode on fresh sync: valid JSON, keys `{copied, skipped, src, dst}` present, `copied == ["a.txt"]`.
  - `--json --dry-run`: `copied` lists would-be files; dst remains empty (no manifest written).
  - Human mode unchanged: output is one path per line, no braces.
  - Idempotency: second `--json` run has `copied == []`.

  - Define `src` and `dst` as the supplied CLI argument strings.

- **Risks:** A second scan can produce inconsistent counts or unnecessary overhead. Changing the public return type would break internal callers. Existing copy-error handling does not reliably distinguish successful copies from attempted copies.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Approve a concrete control flow and field semantics covering first sync, partial changes, no-op, and dry-run, without adding dependencies or changing existing copy-error semantics.

### 3. Implement JSON reporting
- **Agent:** `impl`
- **Objective:** Add `--json`, produce one JSON object containing `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`, and support its combination with `--dry-run`.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Human-readable paths leaking into JSON stdout; dry-run writing files or manifests; accidental changes to path ordering, exit codes, or the `sync()` API.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Successful JSON invocations produce stdout that parses as exactly one object. Human mode retains one path per line. Combined flags report planned copies without filesystem writes. Existing direct `sync()` calls remain compatible.

### 4. Add contract and regression tests
- **Agent:** `impl`
- **Objective:** Add meaningful tests for the new CLI contract and compatibility.
- **Files:** Modify `test_synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Checking only key presence can miss incorrect counts, extra stdout, or unintended dry-run side effects.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Tests verify:
  - Entire stdout parses as one JSON object with the required keys and correct value types.
  - Initial, partially changed, and no-op syncs return correct paths and skipped counts.
  - JSON dry-run reports planned paths and leaves destination contents and manifest unchanged, including an absent destination.
  - Human output, `sync()`’s list return, and representative CLI exit behavior remain compatible.
  - Tests use Python 3.10 standard-library facilities only.

### 5. Review implementation and tests
- **Agent:** `review`
- **Objective:** Independently inspect the diff and run `python3 -m unittest -v`, comparing results with the baseline.
- **Files:** Review `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none. Corrections return to `impl`.
- **Risks:** Tests can reproduce implementation assumptions and miss contract violations; unrelated baseline failures can obscure release readiness.
- **Acceptance criterion:** No unresolved findings concerning JSON purity, counts, dry-run writes, API compatibility, or exit behavior. The suite has no new failures, and every pre-existing failure is explicitly reported. Any correction receives re-review.

### 6. Document usage and field semantics
- **Agent:** `impl`
- **Objective:** Document human mode, JSON mode, combined dry-run usage, and the meaning of each JSON field.
- **Files:** Modify `README.md`.
- **Risks:** Examples may imply that dry-run copied files or that `skipped` counts failed copies.
- **Acceptance criterion:** README includes runnable examples for `--json` and `--json --dry-run`, a representative JSON object, and wording consistent with the approved semantics.

### 7. Final release verification
- **Agent:** `review`
- **Objective:** Review the complete diff and validate documented CLI examples against the finished implementation.
- **Files:** Review `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Documentation drift, unreviewed follow-up edits, or Python-version incompatibility.
- **Acceptance criterion:** The final reviewed revision passes the added tests on Python 3.10, preserves baseline behavior, has no new test failures, and uses no new dependencies. Any outstanding baseline failure is clearly identified before declaring release readiness.

## Execution plan

The release scope is `--json` output and its interaction with `--dry-run`, while preserving human output, exit behavior, and `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)`.

Repository observations that affect the plan:
- `main()` currently prints the paths returned by `sync()`.
- `sync()` already returns prospective paths without writing during a dry run.
- `skipped` is not defined precisely in `GOAL.md`; its meaning needs an explicit decision.
- `collect_stale()` skips its first entry, which conflicts with existing tests and may cause baseline failures.
- `DESIGN.md` describes a separate daemon proposal; it is outside this release scope.

**Suggested model routing:** `explore` and `impl`: `github-copilot/gpt-6.1-sol medium`; `design-review` and `review`: `github-copilot/gpt-6.1-sol xhigh`.

### 1. Establish the baseline and compatibility surface
**Agent:** `explore`

- **Objective:** Run the existing suite, identify internal `sync()` callers, and record current CLI output, return values, and filesystem effects.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `README.md`, and `GOAL.md`. Create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Existing failures could be mistaken for regressions; internal callers may rely on the list return value as well as the signature.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Report results of `python3 -m unittest -v`, enumerate callers, and document normal, no-op, and dry-run behavior. Classify any failures as pre-existing before implementation.

### 2. Approve the JSON reporting contract
**Agent:** `design-review`

- **Objective:** Resolve reporting semantics and approve a minimal implementation approach.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `GOAL.md`. Create/modify none; return the decision in text.
- **Decisions required:**
  - Define `skipped`; proposed meaning: source files unchanged relative to the stored manifest.
  - Preserve input `src` and `dst` strings in the JSON payload.
  - Preserve the existing `sync()` signature and list return value.
  - Derive copied and skipped results from a consistent source snapshot; avoid an extra scan that can disagree with the sync decision.
  - Clarify how the single-object requirement applies to help, argument errors, and existing runtime failures while preserving exit behavior.
- **Risks:** Counting excluded or failed files as skipped; falsely claiming successful copies when existing copy errors are swallowed; introducing a new public return type.
- **Acceptance criterion:** A written contract covers first sync, mixed changed/unchanged files, no-op, empty source, combined flags, and error/help behavior, with an approved way to obtain counts without breaking callers.

### 3. Implement JSON output and document usage
**Agent:** `impl`

- **Objective:** Add `--json`, emit the agreed payload, and document normal and dry-run usage.
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py`, `README.md`. No new files expected.
- **Implementation requirements:** Use Python 3.10 standard library only; keep JSON rendering at the CLI boundary; introduce private helpers only if needed to share sync results.
- **Risks:** Human-mode output changing, extra stdout text corrupting JSON, dry runs writing files or manifests, or repeated scans producing inconsistent counts.
- **Acceptance criterion:**
  - Successful JSON invocations emit exactly one object with `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`.
  - Human mode remains one relative path per line.
  - `sync()` retains its signature and list return behavior.
  - README examples cover `--json` and `--json --dry-run`.

### 4. Add behavioral regression tests
**Agent:** `impl` — reuse the implementation session

- **Objective:** Verify the output contract and filesystem behavior through CLI-entry-point tests.
- **Files to modify:** `test_synccli.py`.
- **Coverage:**
  - Parse the complete captured stdout with `json.loads`; verify keys, types, paths, and values.
  - First sync, mixed changed/unchanged files, no-op, and empty source.
  - Both flags together list prospective copies and leave destination contents and manifest unchanged, including an initially absent destination.
  - Human output and successful return code remain unchanged.
  - A filename requiring JSON escaping round-trips correctly.
- **Risks:** Tests checking only mocked payloads may miss output contamination or filesystem writes; timestamp-sensitive fixtures may be flaky.
- **Acceptance criterion:** New tests pass using deterministic fixtures. The full suite has no new failures; any baseline failures from Task 1 are explicitly reported.

### 5. Independently review the implementation
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Audit the diff against the approved contract and verify compatibility and test quality.
- **Files:** Review changes in `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`; inspect `incremental.py` for compatibility. Create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Tests may mirror implementation mistakes; skipped counts may use a different snapshot; unrelated daemon or helper fixes may enter the patch.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Every release requirement has code and test evidence, with no unresolved blocking findings. Minor findings return to the same implementation session, followed by re-review; major findings trigger implementation escalation.

### 6. Run the release verification gate
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Verify the final revision after review fixes.
- **Files:** Inspect the final diff and existing files. Create/modify none.
- **Checks:** Run `python3 -m unittest -v` under Python 3.10; smoke-test `python3 -m synccli` in human, JSON, and JSON dry-run modes using temporary fixtures; check `--help`.
- **Risks:** Entry-point behavior may differ from direct `main()` tests; unresolved baseline failures may prevent a green release gate.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Required tests and smoke checks pass, dry-run fixtures remain unchanged, and the final diff contains only approved release changes. Any pre-existing failing tests are a documented release decision rather than silently fixed or ignored.

---

#### Task 1: Interface & Architecture Review
- **Objective:** Finalize the mechanism for tracking and reporting `skipped` file count while maintaining strict backwards compatibility for `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature and return value for existing callers.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only analysis of `synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `DESIGN.md`).
- **Pipeline agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** Altering `sync()`'s return type or signature in a way that breaks internal consumers (e.g., continuous sync daemon or scripts importing `synccli.sync`); ambiguity in defining `skipped` count (e.g., total source files minus copied vs. unreadable files).
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Documented agreement that `sync()` signature remains `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` returning `list[str]`, with `skipped` computed from source manifest count minus copied count, and output formatting cleanly decoupled in `main()`.

---

#### Task 2: Implement `--json` Flag and Output Formatting

- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`

- **Files to create/modify:** `test_synccli.py` (modify).

- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only inspection of `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `GOAL.md`).
- **Pipeline agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** Modifying `sync()` signature or return type, which breaks internal callers; miscalculating `skipped` (e.g. failing to account for total files scanned vs files changed).
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** Formal agreement on how `skipped` is derived (e.g., `len(src_manifest) - len(to_copy)`) and CLI flow ensuring stdout emits only the single JSON object when `--json` is enabled.

---

### Task 2: Implement `--json` Flag and Output Formatting in CLI

Ensure `--dry-run` with `--json` populates `copied` with would-be copied paths, preserve standard line-by-line output in human mode, and preserve exit codes.
- **Files to create/modify:** `synccli.py`
- **Pipeline agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Unintentional stdout pollution (e.g., stray prints or error output directed to stdout instead of stderr) invalidating JSON parsing; altering `sync()` signature.
- **Verifiable acceptance criterion:**

  - `python3 -m synccli --json --dry-run <src> <dst>` lists candidate files in `copied` without creating destination files.

## Execution plan

Based on `GOAL.md`, `README.md`, and the current implementation, this release adds JSON reporting to the existing one-shot CLI.

**Agent/model allocation:** `explore` and `impl`: `github-copilot/gpt-6.1-sol medium`; `design-review` and `review`: `github-copilot/gpt-6.1-sol xhigh`.

### 1. Establish the baseline and reporting contract
**Agent:** `explore`

- **Objective:** Trace argument parsing, file selection, manifest updates, and existing callers of `sync()`. Run the existing test suite and identify baseline failures.
- **Files:** Read `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`; modify none.
- **Risks:** `collect_stale()` currently starts at index 1, apparently conflicting with an existing test. Also, `copy_file()` suppresses some failures, so `sync()` returns selected paths rather than confirmed successful copies.
- **Acceptance criterion:** A baseline report records `python3 -m unittest -v` results, the exact existing `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` contract, and how the CLI currently selects and reports paths.

### 2. Review the minimal JSON-reporting design
**Agent:** `design-review`

- **Objective:** Approve a reporting approach that preserves `sync()`’s signature and list return value. Resolve the unspecified meaning of `skipped`; recommended definition: source files excluded from copying because their recorded state is unchanged.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; modify none. Return the approved design as text.
- **Risks:** Computing `skipped` from the destination manifest after synchronization gives misleading results. Independent rescans can produce inconsistent counts if source files change. `DESIGN.md` concerns a separate v2 daemon and should not expand this release’s scope.
- **Acceptance criterion:** The approved design defines:
  - Exactly the four keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`, including their types and path representation.
  - Consistent file selection/counting for normal and dry-run execution.
  - One JSON object on stdout, preserving existing exit behavior and human output.
  - How shared internal logic avoids changing `sync()`’s external contract.

### 3. Implement the feature, regression tests, and usage documentation
**Agent:** `impl`

- **Objective:** Add `--json`, implement the approved reporting design, and document its interaction with `--dry-run`.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`. Create no new files or dependencies.
- **Risks:** Human path output leaking into JSON mode; dry-run writing files or manifests; changed return values for internal callers; incorrect no-op counts.
- **Acceptance criterion:** New tests in `test_synccli.py` verify:
  - The complete captured stdout parses as one JSON object with the required keys and types.
  - Initial sync reports the expected copied paths and skipped count.
  - Repeated sync reports `copied: []` and the expected unchanged-file count.
  - Mixed changed/unchanged input reports accurate paths and counts.
  - `--json --dry-run` reports would-copy paths without creating or altering destination files or the manifest.
  - Human mode retains one relative path per line, including dry-run.
  - Successful calls retain exit code `0`; direct `sync()` callers retain the signature and list return.
  
  README examples cover JSON output and combined flags. All additions use Python 3.10 standard-library APIs.

### 4. Independently review the implementation
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Audit the complete diff against the approved contract and `GOAL.md`.
- **Files:** Review `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`; modify none.
- **Risks:** Tests can validate JSON syntax while missing extra stdout, filesystem mutations, compatibility changes, or counting inconsistencies.
- **Acceptance criterion:** A written review confirms stdout exclusivity, dry-run behavior, unchanged human output and exit behavior, stable `sync()` contract, and adequate regression coverage. Blocking findings return to the same `impl` agent, followed by re-review.

### 5. Verify release readiness
**Agent:** `review`

- **Objective:** Validate the reviewed changes using the README test command and CLI smoke checks.
- **Files:** Inspect the final diff and changed files; modify none.
- **Risks:** Pre-existing failures being mistaken for regressions, or being silently hidden; CLI behavior differing from directly invoked unit tests.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Record results for:
  - `python3 -m unittest -v`
  - `python3 -m synccli --help`, showing both flags.
  - Module-level CLI checks for human output, JSON output, and JSON dry-run.
  - Python 3.10 compatibility and absence of new dependencies.

  Release readiness requires no new failures. Any baseline failure is explicitly tracked; if it blocks a green release, authorize a separate targeted `impl` fix and `review` cycle.

# Execution Plan: `--json` output mode + `--dry-run` interplay

**Repo state reviewed:** `synccli.py` (103 lines, stdlib only), `test_synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `DESIGN.md`. Key facts: `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` already returns the copied/would-copy list and already supports dry-run without writing the manifest — the goal is almost entirely a `main()` concern plus tests.

---

## Task 0 — Explore: contract discovery (read-only)

- **Agent:** `explore`
- **Objective:** Confirm (a) all internal callers of `sync()` and their expectations of the return value, (b) anything else in the codebase writes to stdout, (c) check `DESIGN.md`/`incremental.py` for a pre-existing definition of "skipped".
- **Files:** none modified.
- **Risks:** none (read-only).
- **Acceptance:** Written summary listing every `sync()` call site and every stdout write site; a proposed definition of `skipped` grounded in existing code.

## Task 1 — Design: output contract (short design + critique)

- **Agent:** `cheap-design`, then `cheap-design-review`
- **Objective:** Pin down the ambiguous parts before code exists:
  1. Definition of `"skipped": N` (most plausible: files present in source but not copied this run — needs Task 0 evidence).
  2. `src`/`dst` values: raw argv strings vs. normalized paths.
  3. JSON emitted via `json.dumps(...)` + single `print`, or `json.dump(sys.stdout)`; ensure exit code stays `0` and argparse errors still go to stderr.
- **Files:** none modified (decision doc in task output only).
- **Risks:** wrong `skipped` semantics silently locked into the public JSON shape.
- **Acceptance:** A one-paragraph spec stating exact JSON key set, value types, and `src`/`dst` formatting, approved by design-review.

## Task 2 — Implement `--json` in `main()`

- **Agent:** `cheap-code`
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py` (only `main()` and the argparse block; **`sync()` signature must not change**).
- **Objective:**
  - Add `parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")`.
  - Build `result = {"copied": copied, "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}` and print exactly one JSON object to stdout when `--json`; human mode keeps one-path-per-line, unchanged.
  - With `--dry-run` + `--json`: `copied` = would-be list (already what `sync(dry_run=True)` returns — verify, don't regress).
- **Risks:** printing anything extra to stdout in JSON mode (violates "nothing else"); accidentally changing `sync()` signature; computing `skipped` requires info `sync()` doesn't return (may need to derive from `build_manifest` count — but re-scanning duplicates work; design doc from Task 1 decides).
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` emits one parseable JSON object with all 4 keys; `--json --dry-run` lists would-be files; human output byte-identical to before.

## Task 3 — Unit tests

- **Agent:** `cheap-code` (same instance as Task 2 via `task_id`, or fresh if large gap)
- **Files to modify:** `test_synccli.py` (new test class, e.g. `TestJsonOutput`).
- **Objective:** Cover: valid JSON (parse stdout), all keys present with correct types, dry-run semantics (`copied` populated, **no files written, no manifest created** in dst), JSON + real run equality of `copied`, exit code 0 in all modes, human mode still prints bare paths.
- **Risks:** mtime granularity flakiness in sync tests (existing pattern works — reuse `tempfile.mkdtemp` setup); capturing stdout needs `contextlib.redirect_stdout` (stdlib, no new deps).
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes; new tests fail against un-patched `synccli.py` (proves they test the feature).

## Task 4 — Review + verification

- **Agent:** `cheap-review` (mandatory), escalate to `strong-code` only on major findings; final audit `strong-review` if warranted.
- **Objective:** Verify constraints: stdlib-only imports, `sync()` untouched, stdout purity in JSON mode, no behavior change in human mode, dry-run writes nothing.
- **Risks:** reviewer misses stdout-purity edge (e.g., a future warning landing on stdout).
- **Acceptance:** Review returns zero major findings; full suite green; manual spot-check of the 4 CLI mode combinations.

### 1. Establish the baseline and trace callers
- **Agent:** `explore`
- **Objective:** Trace CLI output, `sync()` callers, manifest handling, and current test results.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Existing failures could be mistaken for regressions. `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, which appears inconsistent with its first-item test.
- **Acceptance:** Report all `sync()` call sites, existing stdout producers, and baseline test results using `python3 -B -m unittest -v`. Record pre-existing failures separately.

### 2. Review the output contract and implementation approach
- **Agent:** `design-review`
- **Objective:** Approve a minimal approach before implementation:
  - Define `skipped` as source-manifest entries requiring no copy.
  - Preserve `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` and its list return value.
  - Obtain copy candidates and skipped count from the same source snapshot, preferably through a private planning helper shared by `sync()` and the CLI.
  - Emit one JSON object on successful JSON-mode execution; preserve existing error and exit behavior.
  - Define `src` and `dst` as the supplied argument strings.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py` and `DESIGN.md`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** `skipped` and path representation are unspecified in the goal. Rescanning can produce inconsistent counts. Swallowed copy errors mean returned paths are not proof of successful writes. The continuous-sync proposal in `DESIGN.md` introduces a separate scope.
- **Acceptance:** A written verdict resolves those ambiguities and approves an approach with no new dependencies, public API changes, or daemon implementation.

### 3. Implement JSON output and regression tests
- **Agent:** `impl`
- **Objective:** Add `--json`, serialize the approved result shape, and retain the existing human-output branch. Add meaningful CLI-level unit tests.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Extra stdout text, incorrect skipped counts, dry-run writes, altered return values, and changed human output.
- **Acceptance:**
  - Captured stdout parses with `json.loads()` as one object containing `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`, with the expected types and values.
  - First sync, unchanged second sync, and mixed changed/unchanged files produce correct counts.
  - `--json --dry-run` lists prospective copies and leaves destination contents and manifest unchanged, including when the destination does not exist.
  - Human mode still prints exactly one relative path per line and nothing for a no-op.
  - Successful `main()` calls still return `0`; `sync()` retains its signature and list return value.
  - No new failures relative to the recorded baseline.

### 4. Independently review the implementation
- **Agent:** `review`
- **Objective:** Audit the implementation and tests against the approved contract and every requirement in `GOAL.md`.
- **Files:** Inspect the diff in `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Tests may validate formatting without proving filesystem behavior, or miss compatibility changes.
- **Acceptance:** Review confirms stdout isolation, dry-run non-mutation, accurate counts, API compatibility, and stdlib-only Python 3.10 support. Findings return to the same `impl` agent for fixes, followed by re-review.

### 5. Document the CLI contract

- **Files:** Modify `README.md`.

- **Pipeline Agent:** `explore`

- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion:** Documented specification defining CLI arguments, `skipped` computation logic, and verification that `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` signature and return behavior remain unchanged.

---

### Task 2: Architecture & Output Contract Review
- **Objective:** Review the planned implementation strategy for `--json` and `--dry-run` to ensure stdout isolation (only the single JSON object is emitted to stdout; errors/logs to stderr if any) and verify backwards compatibility.
- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only review).
- **Pipeline Agent:** `design-review`
- **Risks:** Output pollution on stdout from secondary functions or unhandled exceptions violating the strict machine-readable contract.
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion:** Review approval confirming that `--json` execution writes strictly one valid JSON payload to stdout, preserves standard exit codes, and leaves human-mode output unchanged.

---

### Task 3: Implementation of `--json` Flag and Machine-Readable Formatting
- **Objective:** Add `--json` flag to argument parsing in `synccli.py`, format output as `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}`, support `--dry-run` interplay (listing would-be copied files in `copied`), keep human mode output unchanged, and document the flag in `README.md`.
- **Files to create/modify:** `synccli.py`, `README.md`.
- **Pipeline Agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Unintended stdout pollution; regression in human mode line-by-line output; miscalculating `skipped` when `--dry-run` is active; violating the constraint of Python 3.10 standard library only (no third-party dependencies).
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion:** Running `python3 -m synccli --json <src> <dst>` prints exclusively a single valid JSON object with keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`; running with both `--json` and `--dry-run` populates `copied` without modifying `<dst>`; human mode prints relative paths line by line.

---

### Task 4: Test Suite Expansion for JSON Mode & Dry-Run Interplay
- **Objective:** Add unit tests to `test_synccli.py` validating `--json` output structure, payload keys, dry-run semantics with `--json`, no-op sync behavior (`copied: []`), and stdout purity.
- **Files to create/modify:** `test_synccli.py`.
- **Pipeline Agent:** `impl`
- **Risks:** Tests not isolating stdout capture properly; missing edge cases (e.g., empty source directories, unchanged files counted under `skipped`).
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes cleanly with new unit tests asserting:
  1. Valid JSON output parsable by `json.loads`.
  2. All required keys (`copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`) present with expected types.
  3. `--dry-run --json` correctly lists would-be copied files and leaves destination unchanged.
  4. Human mode remains unaffected.

---

- **Objective:** Perform an independent verification of the diff against all constraints in `GOAL.md` (no external dependencies, Python 3.10 stdlib only, stable `sync()` signature, stdout purity).
- **Files to create/modify:** None (read-only review).

- **Risks:** Subtle signature changes to `sync()` or undocumented regressions escaping into the final release.
- **Verifiable Acceptance Criterion:** Review sign-off confirming 100% compliance with `GOAL.md` constraints, zero extraneous stdout output in JSON mode, and all unit tests passing.

# Execution Plan — `--json` output mode + dry-run interplay

Repo state reviewed: `synccli.py` (103 lines, `sync()` at synccli.py:72, `main()` at synccli.py:90), `test_synccli.py`, no packaging — flat module. All constraints from GOAL.md respected below.

---

## Task 1 — Design the JSON contract and the `sync()` signature decision
- **Objective:** Pin down the exact semantics before code: (a) definition of `skipped` (proposed: total files scanned in src minus `len(copied)` — unchanged files), (b) how to get `skipped` without touching `sync()`'s stable signature — propose a new pure helper (e.g. `count_files(root)` reusing `scan_files`) called from `main()`, or a new `sync_report()` wrapper that `sync()` delegates to, (c) error/exit-code behavior in JSON mode (GOAL says "exit code unchanged" → always 0 today, errors silently swallowed by `copy_file`).
- **Files:** none created; decision recorded in the task brief for Task 2 (optionally a short section in `DESIGN.md` if the team wants it documented).
- **Agent:** `design-review` (read-only critique of the proposed contract).
- **Risks:** `skipped` ambiguity (does it count hidden dirs? excluded files?); `copy_file` swallows `OSError` (synccli.py:68-69), so `copied` can contain files that were NOT actually copied — JSON would lie. Design must decide: document it, or fix the swallow (out of scope per GOAL — recommend documenting).
- **Acceptance criterion:** A written contract stating the exact JSON schema, the `skipped` formula, and that `sync()`'s signature `(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` is untouched; design-review sign-off with no major findings.

## Task 2 — Implement `--json` flag in the CLI
- **Objective:** Add `parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")`; in `main()`, when `--json`: compute `skipped = len(scan_files(src)) - len(copied)` (per Task 1 contract) and `print(json.dumps({"copied": copied, "skipped": skipped, "src": args.src, "dst": args.dst}))` as the ONLY stdout output; human path stays `print(rel)` per line. `--dry-run` needs zero changes — it already returns the would-copy list from `sync()`.
- **Files:** modify `synccli.py` only (imports already have `json`).
- **Agent:** `cheap-code` (impl) — small, mechanical once the contract is fixed.
- **Risks:** stray `print` leaking into stdout in JSON mode (breaks "single JSON object"); `src`/`dst` values — use the raw args as given; accidental change to `sync()` signature (internal callers exist — `incremental.py` must keep working).
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` prints exactly one parseable JSON line with all 4 keys; `--json --dry-run` lists would-be-copied files and writes NO manifest to dst; `python3 -m synccli src dst` output byte-identical to before.

## Task 3 — Unit tests
- **Objective:** Add to `test_synccli.py`: (1) `--json` emits valid JSON with exactly keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, `dst`; (2) `skipped` correct on second run (0 copied → skipped == file count); (3) `--json --dry-run`: `copied` = would-copy list, dst remains empty / no manifest created; (4) human mode regression (one path per line).
- **Files:** modify `test_synccli.py` only.
- **Agent:** `cheap-code` (impl), same instance as Task 2 via `task_id` resume (shared context).
- **Risks:** stdout capture must intercept prints (`contextlib.redirect_stdout` or `unittest.mock.patch`); flaky mtimes if tests reuse fixtures — build fresh temp dirs per test.
- **Acceptance criterion:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes with the new tests green and zero existing tests broken.

## Task 4 — Mandatory review of the full diff
- **Objective:** Independent review of Tasks 2–3: signature stability, stdout purity in JSON mode, no new imports/deps, Python 3.10 stdlib only.
- **Files:** none (read-only).
- **Agent:** `cheap-review` (mandatory per pipeline, even on a short diff). Minor findings → back to Task 2 instance; major → escalate to `strong-code`.
- **Risks:** reviewer missing the cross-module impact on `incremental.py` — explicitly instruct it to check that file's use of `sync()`.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Review returns no major findings; all minors fixed and re-verified.

## Task 5 — Final verification (audit)
- **Objective:** End-to-end check against GOAL.md constraints: run full suite, run CLI manually in both modes on a temp fixture, confirm `sync()` signature unchanged (`git diff` shows no change at synccli.py:72), no new dependencies.
- **Files:** none modified.
- **Agent:** `strong-review` (final audit, strongest rung).
- **Risks:** none new; this is the safety net.
- **Acceptance criterion:** GOAL.md items 1–4 each demonstrably satisfied (JSON single-object stdout, dry-run interplay, human mode unchanged, tests present and passing); audit verdict recorded.

  * Ambiguity in `skipped` definition (total scanned source files minus copied files).

---

#### **Task 2: CLI Implementation (`--json` flag and output handler)**

* **Files to create/modify:** `synccli.py`
* **Pipeline agent:** `impl` (`cheap-code`)
* **Risks:** 
  * Extraneous prints polluting stdout in JSON mode.
  * Inconsistent path types (must serialize `src` and `dst` cleanly as strings).
  * Regression in exit codes or human-readable output format.
* **Verifiable acceptance criterion:** 
  * Running `python3 -m synccli <src> <dst> --json` outputs exactly one valid JSON object with keys `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`.
  * Running `python3 -m synccli <src> <dst> --json --dry-run` reports potential copies without modifying the destination or writing manifest.

---

#### **Task 3: Unit & Integration Test Suite Extension**

* **Files to create/modify:** `test_synccli.py`

  1. `--json` produces parseable JSON with all required keys.
  2. `--json` + `--dry-run` reflects intended copies while destination remains untouched.
  3. Non-zero `skipped` count is accurate when destination is partially or fully up-to-date.
  4. Human mode remains unaffected.

## Execution plan

The release target in `GOAL.md` is JSON output with dry-run support, unchanged human output, Python 3.10 standard-library compatibility, and a stable `sync()` signature.

I read `GOAL.md`, `README.md`, and the implementation/tests to ground the plan. No files were modified and no tests were run.

**Agent roles:** `explore` = exploration agent; `impl` = `cheap-code`; `review` = `cheap-review`; `design-review` = `cheap-design-review`. Each implementation task must pass review before dependent work proceeds.

### 1. Establish the baseline and output contract
**Agent:** `explore`

- **Objective:** Trace `main()` → `sync()`, locate internal callers, and establish existing test results. Define `skipped` as the number of source-manifest entries that do not require copying; define `src` and `dst` as the supplied CLI argument strings.
- **Files:** Read `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, `README.md`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** `GOAL.md` does not explicitly define `skipped`. `copy_file()` suppresses some failures, so the current returned list represents selected paths rather than verified successful copies.
- **Acceptance criterion:** A read-only report identifies callers, confirms signature and return-value compatibility requirements, records the test baseline, and documents the proposed JSON field semantics.

**Known baseline issue:** `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, which conflicts with `test_first_item_stale_is_included`. Confirm and track this existing failure separately so later verification can distinguish regressions.

### 2. Review the result-sharing design
**Agent:** `design-review`  
**Depends on:** Task 1

- **Objective:** Approve a minimal way for the CLI to obtain copied paths and skipped count from the same synchronization snapshot. Prefer a private result-producing helper, with `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` retaining its existing list return.
- **Files:** Read `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none. Return the design decision as text.
- **Risks:** A second source scan can introduce inconsistent counts and unnecessary work. Changing `sync()`’s return type would break callers. Broad copy-error changes would alter existing behavior.
- **Acceptance criterion:** The approved design preserves `sync()`’s signature and list return, uses one source-manifest snapshot per invocation, defines skipped-count arithmetic, and preserves existing exit/error behavior.

### 3. Implement JSON output and synchronization result plumbing
**Agent:** `impl`  
**Depends on:** Task 2

- **Objective:** Add `--json`, expose the approved internal result, and select JSON or human rendering in `main()`.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Extra stdout messages corrupt machine-readable output; duplicate execution can copy twice; early returns can change dry-run behavior.
- **Acceptance criterion:**
  - Successful JSON invocations emit exactly one object with `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`.
  - `copied` is a list of relative paths; `skipped` is an integer.
  - `--json --dry-run` reports would-copy paths without creating or changing destination files or the manifest.
  - Human mode retains one path per line and empty stdout for a no-op.
  - `sync()` retains its signature and list return; exit behavior remains unchanged.
  - No dependencies are added.

### 4. Add behavioral regression tests
**Agent:** `impl` — reuse Task 3’s implementation agent  
**Depends on:** Task 3

- **Objective:** Verify the JSON contract and compatibility using temporary directories and captured CLI output.
- **Files:** Modify `test_synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Shape-only assertions can miss wrong counts or dry-run writes. Tests that mock synchronization entirely can miss integration problems.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Tests cover:
  - Parsing the entire stdout with `json.loads`, required keys, types, and exact expected values.
  - First sync, unchanged second sync, and a mixed changed/unchanged source.
  - Combined flags with both a missing destination and an existing destination/manifest, verifying no writes.
  - Exact human output and unchanged direct `sync()` return behavior.
  - CLI return codes and at least one `python3 -m synccli` subprocess invocation.

### 5. Review implementation and tests
**Agent:** `review`  
**Depends on:** Task 4

- **Objective:** Independently check correctness, compatibility, stdout isolation, and test coverage.
- **Files:** Read the diff in `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`; create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Tests and implementation may share the same mistaken skipped-count assumption; hidden API changes may escape CLI tests.
- **Acceptance criterion:** No unresolved findings concerning the release contract. Minor findings return to the original `impl` agent for fixes and re-review; major findings escalate to `strong-code`, followed by independent review.

### 6. Document the user-facing behavior
**Agent:** `impl`  
**Depends on:** Task 5

- **Objective:** Add concise usage examples and explain JSON fields and dry-run behavior.
- **Files:** Modify `README.md`.
- **Risks:** Documentation may imply verified copy success beyond existing behavior or describe counts inconsistently.
- **Acceptance criterion:** README includes examples for `--json` and `--json --dry-run`, the four-field schema, skipped-count semantics, and the existing test command. Examples match reviewed behavior.

### 7. Perform final release verification
**Agent:** `review`
