- **Risks:** a red baseline (expected: `collect_stale` bug) masks regressions from Task 2/3.
- **Acceptance:** report lists suite pass/fail result, every `sync(` call site, and confirmation that only stdlib is used.

## Task 1 — Design decision: JSON shape & API boundaries
- **Objective:** Settle the two open design questions before code: (a) semantics of `"skipped"` (propose: files in src manifest that were unchanged, i.e. not copied this run); (b) how to expose skipped count without breaking `sync()`'s stable signature (propose: new internal `sync_detailed(src_root, dst_root, dry_run) -> dict`, with `sync()` kept as a thin wrapper returning `["copied"]` — zero change for `incremental.py`/future `syncd`).
- **Files:** none modified (decision recorded in the task brief for Task 2).
- **Agent:** `design-review`.
- **Risks:** wrong `skipped` semantics violates GOAL's implied contract; wrapper duplication drift between `sync()` and `sync_detailed()`.
- **Acceptance:** written decision specifying skipped semantics, dry-run interplay (with `--dry-run`, `copied` = would-be-copied, unchanged behavior), and stdout purity rule ("nothing else on stdout in `--json` mode, exit code unchanged").

## Task 2 — Implementation (CLI + core)
- **Objective:** Implement `--json` and the detailed-result plumbing.
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py` (add `--json` argparse flag; add `sync_detailed()`; refactor `sync()` to delegate; branch output in `main()`).
- **Agent:** `impl` (cheap-code).
- **Risks:** (1) changing `sync()` signature — forbidden; (2) stdout pollution in JSON mode (any stray `print`, warning, or debug output breaks consumers); (3) `json.dumps` key order/content drift from spec `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}`.
- **Acceptance (verifiable):**
  - `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` prints exactly one line of valid JSON with the four keys; exit code 0.
  - Combined with `--dry-run`, `copied` lists would-be files and no files are created in dst (no manifest written).
  - Without `--json`, output is byte-identical to today (one path per line).

## Task 3 — Unit tests

- **Files to modify:** `test_synccli.py` (new `TestJsonOutput` class).
- **Agent:** `impl` (cheap-code, same instance as Task 2 via `task_id` — shares context).
- **Risks:** flaky manifest comparison via mtime granularity on fast filesystems (second-sync no-op tests already exist and set the pattern); tests calling `main()` with `argv` and capturing stdout must not depend on cwd.

## Execution plan

Target: the JSON output and dry-run behavior in `GOAL.md`, using Python 3.10’s standard library and preserving `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` and its list return value.

The current CLI prints the paths returned by `sync()`. It does not expose a skipped count. `DESIGN.md` describes a separate continuous-sync proposal; this release plan targets the one-shot CLI.

### 1. Establish the baseline and compatibility surface
**Agent:** `explore` — read-only

- **Objective:** Trace `main()`, `sync()`, manifest handling, and internal callers; run the existing test suite and record baseline results.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`. Create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Existing failures could be mistaken for feature regressions. In particular, `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, while an existing test expects the first stale item to be included.
- **Acceptance:** Produce a baseline report with `python3 -m unittest -v` results, caller locations, current stdout/exit behavior, and confirmed dry-run filesystem behavior.

### 2. Review the output contract and implementation approach
**Agent:** `design-review` — read-only

- **Objective:** Approve a minimal approach for obtaining copy paths and skipped counts without changing the public `sync()` contract.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py` and `test_synccli.py`. Create/modify none.
- **Proposed contract:**
  - `copied`: the existing ordered list of relative paths; planned paths during dry-run.
  - `skipped`: source files unchanged according to the existing manifest comparison.
  - `src` and `dst`: the supplied CLI argument strings.
  - Successful JSON-mode execution emits one JSON object and no other stdout.
- **Proposed implementation:** Share the existing synchronization logic through a private helper that also returns the skipped count. Keep `sync()` as a compatible list-returning wrapper; let `main()` select the output format.
- **Risks:** A second source scan can produce inconsistent counts. Existing copy-error handling can report planned paths as copied. Help/error output needs an explicit interpretation of the JSON contract.
- **Acceptance:** Reviewer approves the count semantics, compatibility guarantees, and stdout behavior, including how `--help` and argument failures interact with `--json`. The approach uses one source snapshot per operation and adds no dependencies.

### 3. Implement the feature, regression tests, and usage documentation
**Agent:** `impl` → `cheap-code`

- **Objective:** Add `--json`, preserve human output, and implement the reviewed contract.
- **Files to modify:** `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `README.md`.
- **Risks:** Accidental changes to `sync()` arguments or return type; extra stdout; dry-run writing files or manifests; incorrectly counting unchanged files.
- **Acceptance:**
  - Captured stdout parses in its entirety with `json.loads()`.
  - Output has `copied`, `skipped`, `src`, and `dst`, with the agreed values and types.
  - Tests cover initial copy, unchanged second run, mixed changed/unchanged files, and an empty source.
  - `--json --dry-run` reports planned paths and leaves destination files and manifest unchanged, including when the destination does not yet exist.
  - Human mode still prints exactly one copied/planned relative path per line.
  - Existing direct `sync()` calls retain their signature and list result.
  - Successful `main()` and subprocess invocation retain exit code 0; existing error exit behavior is preserved.
  - README includes JSON and combined dry-run examples.
  - Feature tests pass using stdlib-only `unittest`.

### 4. Independently review the implementation
**Agent:** `review` → `cheap-review`

- **Objective:** Audit the complete diff against `GOAL.md` and the approved contract.
- **Files:** Review changes to `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`. Create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Tests may validate only parsing rather than actual synchronization semantics, or miss filesystem side effects.
- **Acceptance:** Review explicitly checks signature compatibility, single-object stdout, correct skipped counts, dry-run immutability, human-mode compatibility, Python 3.10 support, and meaningful test coverage. No unresolved findings remain before proceeding.

### 5. Resolve findings and baseline blockers
**Agent:** `impl`, followed by `review`

- **Objective:** Fix review findings and resolve any prerequisite for a green release suite.
- **Files:** Only files implicated by findings. A baseline `collect_stale()` repair, if confirmed and included in release scope, belongs in `synccli.py` and uses the existing regression test.
- **Risks:** Expanding a focused output feature into unrelated synchronization fixes.
- **Acceptance:** Minor findings return to the same implementation agent and are re-reviewed. Major findings escalate to `strong-code` with independent review. Any baseline repair is separately identified and verified; feature changes introduce no new failures.

### 6. Verify the release candidate
**Agent:** `review` — final read-only verification

- **Objective:** Confirm the integrated CLI behavior and documentation.
- **Files:** Inspect the final feature files. Create/modify none.
- **Risks:** Unit-level success may conceal module-entry-point or output-stream problems.
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m unittest -v` passes, and subprocess checks using `python3 -m synccli` confirm JSON output, human output, dry-run behavior, help behavior, and exit codes. Any unresolved baseline failure is reported as a release blocker.

+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged
[0m

"""synccli: synchronize a source directory into a destination using a manifest."""
import argparse
import json
import os
import sys

MANIFEST_NAME = ".sync_manifest.json"


def scan_files(root):
    """Return sorted list of relative file paths under root."""
    result = []
    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
        dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if not d.startswith(".")]
        for name in filenames:
            full = os.path.join(dirpath, name)
            rel = os.path.relpath(full, root)
            result.append(rel)
    return sorted(result)


def file_state(path):
    """Return (size, mtime) for a file."""
    st = os.stat(path)
    return {"size": st.st_size, "mtime": st.st_mtime}


def build_manifest(root):
    """Build a manifest {relpath: state} for all files under root."""
    manifest = {}
    for rel in scan_files(root):
        manifest[rel] = file_state(os.path.join(root, rel))
    return manifest


def changed_files(old_manifest, new_manifest):
    """Return files whose state changed or that are new in new_manifest."""
    changed = []
    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
        if rel not in old_manifest or old_manifest[rel] != state:
            changed.append(rel)
    return changed


def collect_stale(items, seen=None):
    """Collect stale entries: items not yet marked fresh.

    Args:
        items: iterable of (path, fresh) tuples.
        seen: set of paths already marked fresh; defaults to a fresh set.

- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Accidentally changing the `sync()` return type, duplicating synchronization work, contaminating stdout, or introducing filesystem writes during dry-run.
- **Acceptance criterion:**
  - A successful `--json` invocation produces stdout that parses completely with `json.loads()`.
  - All four fields follow the approved contract.
  - `--json --dry-run` reports planned copies without changing destination files or the manifest.
  - Existing direct `sync()` calls retain their signature and list result.
  - No new dependencies or Python features newer than 3.10 are introduced.

+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged

**Defects in `incremental.py` (commit d65c815):**

1. **Major — `copy_many` reports failures as success, silently.** Two layers of swallowing: `copy_file` (synccli.py:66-69) already catches `OSError` internally and returns normally after truncating `dst` to a partial/empty file (a failed mid-read destroys pre-existing `dst` content), and `copy_many`'s own `except Exception: continue` hides the rest. So a dst path ends up in the returned `done` list even though nothing was copied — the docstring contract ("return the list of dst paths done") is false, and per DESIGN ("log to stdout and continue") nothing is logged. Result: destination silently stale or corrupted with no signal to the caller.

2. **Major — `merge_manifest` drops legitimate updates.** The guard `old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]` rejects the new state whenever the old mtime is equal or newer, ignoring `size` entirely. A file rewritten with identical mtime (sub-second edits, restore from archive, coarse filesystem timestamps) but different content/size is rejected forever — the change never propagates and no later sync fixes it, since the stored old state is preserved. This also contradicts the rest of the system: `changed_files` compares full `(size, mtime)` state.

3. **Minor — `filter_excluded` mutable default argument.** `excluded=[]` is created once at definition time; every call without an explicit list appends to the same shared list and returns it. The second call in a process returns the first call's paths again, so callers acting on the result can exclude/skip paths that aren't in the current set.

4. **Minor — `merge_manifest` never removes deleted entries.** It's a pure union: relpaths present in `old` but absent from `new_manifest` are carried forward forever, so files deleted from the source stay pinned as "known" in the destination manifest and deletions can never be detected or propagated.

### Task 1: Codebase Exploration & Contract Analysis

- **Agent:** `explore`
- **Objective:** Trace CLI output, manifest handling, and internal `sync()` callers; run the existing tests before implementation.
- **Files:** Inspect `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, `incremental.py`, `GOAL.md`, and `README.md`. No changes.
- **Risks:** Existing failures could be mistaken for feature regressions. In particular, `collect_stale()` starts at index 1, while an existing test expects the first stale item to be included.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Report the baseline test results, current stdout/exit behavior, and callers that depend on `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` returning a list.

### 2. Review the JSON output contract
- **Agent:** `design-review`
- **Objective:** Resolve reporting semantics before implementation:
  - `copied`: the relative paths returned by `sync()`, including planned paths during dry-run.
  - `skipped`: source files unchanged according to the manifest, rather than copy failures.
  - `src` and `dst`: the supplied CLI arguments.
  - Successful JSON-mode output: exactly one serialized object followed by a newline.
  - Preserve existing exit behavior and specify how help, argument errors, and runtime failures interact with JSON mode.
- **Files:** Inspect `GOAL.md`, `synccli.py`, and `test_synccli.py`. No changes.
- **Risks:** `GOAL.md` does not define `skipped` precisely or provide an error-object schema. `copy_file()` currently suppresses some errors, so returned paths do not establish successful copying. Computing counts with another scan could introduce inconsistent snapshots.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Approve a concrete implementation approach that preserves the `sync()` signature and list return value, uses Python 3.10 standard-library facilities only, and explicitly addresses count consistency and error behavior.

### 3. Implement CLI reporting and document usage
- **Agent:** `impl`
- **Objective:** Add `--json`, select JSON versus human rendering in `main()`, and obtain reporting counts through the approved internal approach. Add concise normal and dry-run examples to the README.
- **Files:** Modify `synccli.py` and `README.md`.
- **Risks:** Path lines leaking into JSON stdout; accidental changes to human output, copying, manifest writes, or internal caller compatibility.
- **Acceptance criterion:**
  - `python3 -m synccli SRC DST --json` emits the four required keys with the approved types and values.
  - With `--json --dry-run`, `copied` lists planned changes and no files or manifest are written.
  - Human mode still prints one relative path per line.
  - `sync()` retains its exact signature and list return contract.
  - Help lists `--json`; no dependency is introduced.

### 4. Add feature and regression tests
- **Agent:** `impl`
- **Objective:** Exercise the CLI boundary and filesystem effects using temporary directories and captured output.
- **Files:** Modify `test_synccli.py`.
- **Risks:** Tests that only check key presence could miss trailing stdout, incorrect counts, or dry-run writes. Filesystem timestamp granularity could make changed-file tests unreliable.
- **Acceptance criterion:** Tests verify:
  - The **entire captured stdout** parses as one JSON object.
  - Required keys, value types, relative paths, and source/destination values.
  - First sync, unchanged second sync, mixed changed/unchanged files, and empty source.
  - Dry-run reports planned paths while preserving destination contents and manifest bytes; it also leaves an absent destination absent.
  - Human output and successful exit codes remain unchanged.
  - Fixtures clean up temporary resources and avoid timing-dependent assertions.

### 5. Review and verify the complete change
- **Agent:** `review`
- **Objective:** Independently inspect the implementation, tests, and documentation; run the release checks.
- **Files:** Review `synccli.py`, `test_synccli.py`, and `README.md`. Review is read-only; corrections return to `impl`, followed by re-review.
- **Risks:** Unrelated fixes expanding scope, insufficient tests for stdout exclusivity, or a green feature subset hiding baseline failures.
- **Acceptance criterion:**
  - Run `python3 -m unittest -v`, preferably under Python 3.10.
  - Smoke-test the documented module invocation in human, JSON, and JSON dry-run modes.
  - Confirm every `GOAL.md` requirement has verification evidence.
  - Report any baseline failures separately; a failing full suite must not be reported as passing.
  - Approve only after review findings are resolved or explicitly recorded as blockers.

**Scope boundary:** `DESIGN.md` describes a separate continuous-sync daemon proposal; it is not required for this release. Any baseline bug repair should be separately scoped before implementation.

    v0.2: add exclude filter, batch copy and manifest merge

diff --git a/incremental.py b/incremental.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..8c1c17d
--- /dev/null
+++ b/incremental.py
@@ -0,0 +1,40 @@
+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged
[0m

# Execution plan — `--json` output mode + dry-run UX

## Repo facts (from recon)
- `synccli.py:72` `sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False)` returns `to_copy` (list of rel paths) in both modes; dry-run returns before copying/writing manifest (`synccli.py:81`).
- `main()` (`synccli.py:90`) prints one path per line and returns `0`. No `--json` yet.
- `incremental.py` imports `copy_file` from `synccli`; no other module calls `sync()` in-repo — the "internal callers" the goal mentions are outside this snapshot, so the signature must not change.
- Tests: `python3 -m unittest -v`; `test_synccli.py` has `TestSync` asserting `sync()` return value directly.
- No project-local pipeline agents (`.opencode/agent/` absent) → use the kit's fallback agents.
- **Key open decision:** `skipped` is not derivable from `sync()`'s return value (it returns only copied). Must be computed without changing `sync()`'s signature.

## Tasks

### T1 — Confirm call surface & test harness (explore)
- **Objective:** Verify no in-repo caller depends on `main()`'s stdout format or on `sync()` return type beyond `TestSync`; confirm exact unittest command; confirm Python version available.
- **Files:** none modified (read-only).
- **Agent:** `explore`.
- **Risks:** external callers not visible in snapshot; assume stable signature anyway.
- **Acceptance:** written list of every in-repo reference to `sync`, `main`, and stdout-printing; confirmation `python3 -m unittest -v` is green on current HEAD.

### T2 — Design the JSON assembly + `skipped` semantics (design → design-review)
- **Objective:** Decide how to produce `{"copied":[...],"skipped":N,"src":...,"dst":...}` while keeping `sync()` stable. Candidate: `main()` calls `scan_files`/`build_manifest` on src and computes `skipped = len(src_manifest) - len(copied)`; or add a new helper `run_sync(...)` returning a structured result that `main()` formats. Also fix semantics of `skipped` (unchanged+excluded files vs only unchanged) and of `src`/`dst` (echo args verbatim vs normalized).
- **Files:** none (design note only, unless team wants `DESIGN.md` appendix).
- **Agent:** `cheap-design` then `cheap-design-review` (real API/UX stakes: stdout contract is externally observable).
- **Risks:** wrong `skipped` definition; double-walking the tree (scan in `main` + in `sync`) causing inconsistency; accidentally changing `sync()` signature.
- **Acceptance:** design-review approves a specific `skipped` definition and an assembly location; explicit statement that `sync(src, dst, dry_run=False)` signature is untouched.

### T3 — Implement `--json` flag + JSON output (impl)
- **Objective:** Add `--json` to the parser; when set, print exactly one JSON object to stdout and nothing else; human mode unchanged (one path per line). Honor T2's decisions; keep exit code `0`.
- **Files:** modify `synccli.py` (parser + `main`, plus any new helper agreed in T2). No changes to `sync()` signature.
- **Agent:** `cheap-code`.
- **Risks:** stray `print`/logging leaking to stdout; `json.dumps` ordering/`ensure_ascii`; dry-run path must still list would-be copies (already true from `sync`); non-ASCII paths.
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m synccli src dst --json` emits one parseable JSON object with all four keys; `python3 -m synccli src dst` output identical to pre-change; `python3 -m synccli src dst --json --dry-run` leaves dst untouched and `copied` lists would-be copies; exit codes unchanged.

### T4 — Add unit tests to `test_synccli.py` (impl)
- **Objective:** Tests for JSON shape: valid JSON, all keys present, `skipped` value correct, dry-run semantics (`copied` = would-be, no files written, manifest not written), and human mode still one-path-per-line. Test `main()` output via `redirect_stdout` / `contextlib`.
- **Files:** modify `test_synccli.py` only.
- **Agent:** `cheap-code` (same session as T3 via `task_id` — shared context on the new output path).
- **Risks:** asserting on `json.loads` vs raw string (must assert single object, no trailing text); mtime/size flakiness in `skipped` assertions.
- **Acceptance:** `python3 -m unittest -v` green; new tests fail if `--json` prints extra text or omits a key (verify by temporary mutation, then revert).

### T5 — Independent review of the diff (review)
- **Objective:** Read-only review of T3+T4 against GOAL.md: stdout purity in JSON mode, unchanged human mode, stable `sync()` signature, no new deps, Python 3.10 stdlib only, test quality.
- **Files:** none (read-only).
- **Agent:** `cheap-review`; escalate to `strong-review` only if a major finding (stdout contamination, signature change, dependency).
- **Risks:** subtle stdout leak from argparse/errors; `skipped` off-by-one on the manifest file itself.
- **Acceptance:** verdict with findings classified minor/major; minor findings routed back to the T3/T4 session via `task_id`.

### T6 — Final verification (review)
- **Objective:** Run `python3 -m unittest -v`; manually run both modes on a temp tree; diff human-mode stdout against `git stash` baseline; confirm exit code `0` and JSON parses.
- **Files:** none (read-only).
- **Agent:** `cheap-review` (or orchestrator directly for the final smoke run).
- **Risks:** none beyond re-running T5 checks.
- **Acceptance:** all four GOAL items demonstrably satisfied; command transcript recorded.

## Ordering & gates
T1 → T2 (design-review gate) → T3 → T4 → T5 (review gate) → T6. T3 and T4 share one `cheap-code` session; T5 must not be skipped even on a short diff. No commits unless explicitly requested.

## Top risks to watch

3. stdout contamination in `--json` mode (T5's main focus).
4. `sync()` signature drift despite the stable-signature constraint (T5).

Here are the defects in commit `0c159b0` (`incremental.py`):

1. **[Major] Mutable default argument corrupts state across calls (`filter_excluded`, line 10)**
   `def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):` uses a mutable list as a default argument and mutates it in place (`excluded.append(p)`). Every call that omits `excluded` shares and accumulates into the same list across invocations, returning stale paths from previous calls (and mutating the caller's list if one is passed).

2. **[Major] Silent failure & false completion reporting in `copy_many` (`copy_many`, lines 18–27)**
   - **Swallowed errors:** `except Exception: continue` silently hides all copy errors (disk full, permission denied, missing source, etc.) with no logging or error reporting.
   - **Broken interaction with `copy_file`:** `synccli.copy_file` already catches `OSError` internally and returns `None` without raising (and if `open(src, "rb")` fails, only `os.makedirs` has run; if `fdst.write` fails mid-write, a truncated/corrupted `dst` file is left behind). Because `copy_file` does not raise on `OSError`, `copy_many` unconditionally executes `done.append(dst)`, falsely reporting failed copies as completed.

3. **[Major] `merge_manifest` does not persist the merged manifest and can corrupt sync state (`merge_manifest`, lines 30–40)**
   - **Misleading contract / unpersisted state:** Despite taking `dst_manifest_path` and stating *"Merge new file states into the destination manifest"*, the function only reads `dst_manifest_path` and returns a dict in memory without ever writing it back to disk.
   - **Silent data/update loss on timestamp comparison:** `old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]` ignores `"size"` changes (unlike `synccli.changed_files` which checks `old_manifest[rel] != state`). A file modified within the same timestamp granularity, restored to an older backup/branch, or touched with an older mtime will have its new state silently discarded. Furthermore, if merged unconditionally from `src_manifest` after a partial failure in `copy_many`, recording `state` for files that failed to copy will prevent future sync passes from retrying them.

4. **[Minor] Misleading API / broken path matching in `filter_excluded` (`EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS` & `filter_excluded`, lines 7–15)**
   - **Name vs. behavior:** `filter_excluded` returns the *excluded* paths rather than filtering them out of `paths`, which is the opposite of standard `filter_*` semantics and easy for callers to invert.
   - **Top-segment splitting pitfalls:** `p.split(os.sep)[0]` fails to match paths normalized with `./` (e.g. `./.git/config` yields `"."`), leading-slash paths (`/.git` yields `""`), or forward-slash paths on Windows (`/` vs `os.sep`), and only checks the root segment so nested `__pycache__` directories (`pkg/__pycache__/mod.pyc`) are never excluded.

Here are the defects in commit `6559395` (`incremental.py`):

1. **[Major] Mutable default argument causes state corruption across calls (`filter_excluded`, line 10)**
   `def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):` uses a mutable list as its default argument and mutates it in-place (`excluded.append(p)`). Every call that omits `excluded` appends to and returns the same shared list, accumulating stale entries from all previous invocations. Additionally, if a caller passes their own list, it is mutated in-place as a side effect.

2. **[Major] Silent failure / false-positive return in `copy_many` (`copy_many`, lines 18–27)**
   `copy_many` wraps `copy_file(src, dst)` in a `try ... except Exception` block intending to only append `dst` to `done` when the copy succeeds. However:
   - `synccli.copy_file` already catches and swallows `OSError` internally (lines 68–69 of `synccli.py`), returning `None` instead of raising when opening/reading/writing fails. Consequently, `copy_many` appends `dst` to `done` even when the file was not actually copied (or when `open(dst, "wb")` truncated an existing destination file before `open(src, "rb")` or `fsrc.read()` failed).
   - Catching bare `Exception` also silently swallows programming errors or unexpected failures without logging or reporting which files failed, leading to silent data loss / incomplete syncs while callers believe the batch succeeded.

3. **[Major] `merge_manifest` does not persist the merged manifest to disk and drops deletions/rollbacks (`merge_manifest`, lines 30–40)**
   - **Misleading API contract / unpersisted state:** The docstring states *"Merge new file states into the destination manifest"* and the first parameter is `dst_manifest_path` (a file path, not a dict), but the function only reads `dst_manifest_path` and returns the in-memory `merged` dict without ever writing it back to `dst_manifest_path`.
   - **Missed updates on mtime ties or clock skew / size changes:** The condition `old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]` ignores `"size"` (unlike `synccli.changed_files`, which compares the full `{"size", "mtime"}` dict). A file modified within the same timestamp granularity or reverted to an older backup with a smaller/older `mtime` will have its new state ignored, leaving the manifest out of sync with actual file contents.
   - **Orphaned entries:** Files deleted from the source (absent from `new_manifest`) remain permanently in `merged` because `merged` starts as `dict(old)`.

4. **[Minor] Incomplete / fragile path matching in `filter_excluded` (`filter_excluded`, lines 7, 13)**
   - `p.split(os.sep)[0]` only checks the first path segment. `__pycache__` directories and `.sync_manifest.json` inside subdirectories (e.g. `pkg/__pycache__/mod.pyc`) will not be excluded.
   - On platforms or callers using normalized/POSIX paths with `/` while `os.sep` is `\` (or leading `./` like `./.git/config`), `p.split(os.sep)[0]` fails to match `EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS`.

[0m→ [0mRead DESIGN.md
### 1. What will break under real load (50,000 files @ 500ms)
* **I/O and CPU thrashing:** `stat()`-ing 50,000 files every 500ms equals **100,000 syscalls/sec**. On macOS/APFS or Windows, walking 50k entries takes hundreds of milliseconds to seconds—your poll cycle will exceed 500ms and pin a core + disk I/O at 100% permanently, draining laptop battery even when idle.
* **Manifest serialization bottleneck:** A 50,000-entry JSON file is **5–10 MB**, not "small". Serializing and writing 10 MB twice a second = **10–20 MB/s of continuous disk writes** (~1.7 TB/day of SSD wear) when zero files have changed.
* **Torn copies & partial writes:** Copying "immediately on detection" means catching files mid-write (e.g., video exports, `git checkout`, compiler outputs, large downloads), syncing corrupted half-written files to `dst`, or failing mid-copy and waiting another full cycle.

### 2. Wrong assumptions & major risks
* **"Atomicity is not needed" (Data Corruption):** Rewriting the manifest in-place without atomic rename (`write to .tmp` + `rename`) guarantees a truncated/corrupt JSON manifest if `syncd` is killed, crashes, or loses power mid-write. Also, copying files to `dst` directly in-place exposes partial files to readers on `dst`.
* **"mtime always changes on edit":** False for `mtime` granularity collisions (rapid writes within the same tick), `mmap` writes on some filesystems, tools that preserve timestamps (`tar`, `cp -p`, `rsync`), or clock skew/NTP adjustments.
* **"If unreadable, log and continue":** If you update the manifest after a failed/skipped copy, the file is never retried. If you don't update the manifest, it spams stdout every 500ms forever.
* **Deletions, renames, and symlinks are unaddressed:**
  * Renaming a 2 GB file or a directory of 5,000 files will trigger a full delete + re-copy unless tracked by inode (`st_ino`/`st_dev`).
  * What happens when a file is deleted in `src`? Does `sync()` delete it in `dst`? If `src` is accidentally unmounted or emptied, does `syncd` wipe `dst` within 500ms?
* **Single-instance locking:** Nothing prevents two `syncd` instances (or manual `synccli` + `syncd`) from running concurrently and corrupting both the manifest and `dst`.

### 3. Decisions to change first (before writing code)
1. **Use OS file watchers + fallback scan:** Use `FSEvents` / `inotify` (via a standard library like `watchdog` or `notify`) with a 100–500ms **debounce window** to let writes settle, plus a periodic full reconciliation scan (e.g., every 10–30 minutes, not 500ms).
2. **Only write state when state changes, and do it atomically:** Never rewrite the manifest if the diff is empty. When it does change, write to `manifest.json.tmp` and `os.replace()` (or use SQLite in WAL mode so single-row updates are O(1) instead of rewriting 10 MB).
3. **Copy atomically to `dst`:** Copy to `<dst>/.syncd-tmp-<name>`, verify size/mtime of `src` didn't change *during* the copy, then atomic `rename()` into place.
4. **Track `(inode, mtime_ns, size)`:** Use nanosecond mtime (`st_mtime_ns`) + `st_size` + `st_ino` to detect rapid edits and handle renames/moves cheaply without re-copying.
5. **Add retry/backoff state per file:** Transient lock/read errors need exponential backoff, not blind 500ms tight-loop retries or silent drops.

[0m✓ [0mReview DESIGN.md architecture[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
### 1. Major Risks
* **Silent data corruption from torn reads & non-atomic writes:** Copying immediately on a 500 ms poll catches files mid-write (before the writer finishes or renames into place). Because neither file copies (`copy_file`) nor manifest updates use temp-file + `os.replace`, crashes or concurrent reads leave corrupted partial files in `dst` and a truncated/corrupt `.synccli.json` (triggering a full 50k-file re-copy on restart).
* **Silent data loss on failed copies:** Rewriting the full manifest from the newly scanned state records the new `mtime`/`size` even if a file failed to copy ("log and continue"). Subsequent polls will see `old == new` and **never retry** the failed file.
* **No deletion or rename handling:** `changed_files()` only iterates `new_manifest`. Deleted or renamed files in `src` are orphaned in `dst` forever.

### 2. Wrong Assumptions
* **"Polling is cheap":** Walking 50,000 files every 500 ms requires ~100,000 `stat` syscalls/sec (and currently `scan_files` + `build_manifest` stats each file twice). In Python, 50k stats + sorting + dict building takes ~200–800 ms on warm cache and seconds on cold cache—consuming a full CPU core continuously and preventing OS disk sleep/battery idle.
* **"The manifest file is small and we rewrite it in place":** 50,000 entries in `indent=2` JSON is ~4–5 MB. Rewriting (and re-reading via `sync()`) it every 500 ms generates **~8–10 MB/s of continuous disk write amplification (~700 GB/day)** even when zero files change.
* **"mtime always changes on edit":** `git checkout`, `cp -p`, `tar -x`, backup restores, and rapid sub-tick writes can preserve or collide on `mtime` + `size`, causing edits to be permanently missed.
* **"Reusing `sync()` directly" works for a daemon:** `synccli.py`'s `copy_file` does `fdst.write(fsrc.read())` (loading entire large files into RAM) and silently swallows `OSError` (`pass`), while `sync()` re-reads and rewrites the JSON manifest on every call.

### 3. What Will Break Under Real Load (50k files, workstation)
1. **CPU & SSD thrashing while idle:** Constant 2 Hz full-tree walks and ~10 MB/s JSON rewrites will peg a core, drain laptop battery, and wear SSDs while only a few files change per minute.
2. **OOM / head-of-line blocking on large files:** Dropping a 4 GB video/ISO into `src` will spike RAM via `fsrc.read()` and stall all change detection for seconds/minutes.
3. **Mid-save race conditions:** IDE saves, compiler outputs, and `git` operations will be copied mid-write or fail with `FileNotFoundError` on ephemeral `.tmp`/lock files.

### 4. Decisions to Change First
1. **Keep state in memory and persist only on change (atomically):** Hold the manifest in RAM; write to disk only when files actually sync (or on a debounced timer), using compact JSON/SQLite via `.tmp` + `os.replace`. Only update a file's manifest entry *after* its copy succeeds.
2. **Use OS file events (FSEvents/inotify via `watchdog`) + slow reconciliation sweep, or at minimum increase/configure poll interval:** Do not poll 50k files at 2 Hz. If avoiding 3rd-party deps, make the poll interval configurable (default ≥ 5–10 s) and use `os.scandir()` (which yields `DirEntry.stat()` without a second syscall).
3. **Stream and stage file copies atomically:** Copy in chunks (`shutil.copyfileobj`) to a hidden staging file in `dst`, verify `stat` of `src` didn't change during the copy (debounce in-flight writes), then `os.replace` into `dst`.
4. **Define deletion semantics and log to `stderr`:** Explicitly handle `old_manifest - new_manifest` deletions, and log errors to `stderr` (keeping `stdout` clean for `--json`/pipes).

+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file

+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded

+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done

+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged

"""synccli: synchronize a source directory into a destination using a manifest."""
import argparse
import json
import os
import sys

MANIFEST_NAME = ".sync_manifest.json"


def scan_files(root):
    """Return sorted list of relative file paths under root."""
    result = []
    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
        dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if not d.startswith(".")]
        for name in filenames:
            full = os.path.join(dirpath, name)
            rel = os.path.relpath(full, root)
            result.append(rel)
    return sorted(result)


def file_state(path):
    """Return (size, mtime) for a file."""
    st = os.stat(path)
    return {"size": st.st_size, "mtime": st.st_mtime}


def build_manifest(root):
    """Build a manifest {relpath: state} for all files under root."""
    manifest = {}
    for rel in scan_files(root):
        manifest[rel] = file_state(os.path.join(root, rel))
    return manifest


def changed_files(old_manifest, new_manifest):
    """Return files whose state changed or that are new in new_manifest."""
    changed = []
    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
        if rel not in old_manifest or old_manifest[rel] != state:
            changed.append(rel)
    return changed


def collect_stale(items, seen=None):
    """Collect stale entries: items not yet marked fresh.

    Args:
        items: iterable of (path, fresh) tuples.
        seen: set of paths already marked fresh; defaults to a fresh set.
    """
    if seen is None:
        seen = set()
    stale = []
    for i in range(1, len(items)):
        path, fresh = items[i]
        if path not in seen and not fresh:
            stale.append(path)
    return stale


def copy_file(src, dst):
    """Copy src to dst, creating parent directories as needed."""
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dst) or ".", exist_ok=True)
    try:
        with open(src, "rb") as fsrc, open(dst, "wb") as fdst:
            fdst.write(fsrc.read())
    except OSError:
        pass  # unreadable source: skip it, next pass will retry


def sync(src_root, dst_root, dry_run=False):
    """Sync src_root into dst_root; return list of copied relative paths."""
    src_manifest = build_manifest(src_root)
    dst_manifest_path = os.path.join(dst_root, MANIFEST_NAME)
    old_manifest = {}
    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            old_manifest = json.load(f)
    to_copy = changed_files(old_manifest, src_manifest)
    if dry_run:
        return to_copy
    for rel in to_copy:
        copy_file(os.path.join(src_root, rel), os.path.join(dst_root, rel))
    with open(dst_manifest_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(src_manifest, f, indent=2)
    return to_copy


def main(argv=None):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="synccli")
    parser.add_argument("src")
    parser.add_argument("dst")
    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    copied = sync(args.src, args.dst, dry_run=args.dry_run)
    for rel in copied:
        print(rel)
    return 0

# Goal: JSON output mode + dry-run UX (next release)

Add to synccli:

1. `--json` flag: machine-readable output. Must print a single JSON object on
   stdout: `{"copied": [...], "skipped": N, "src": ..., "dst": ...}`. Nothing
   else may be printed to stdout in that mode. Exit code unchanged.
2. `--dry-run` interplay: with both flags, `copied` lists what WOULD be copied.
3. Human mode unchanged (one path per line).
4. Tests: unit tests for the JSON shape (valid JSON, keys present, dry-run
   semantics) added to test_synccli.py.

Constraints: no new dependencies, keep Python 3.10 stdlib only, keep
`sync()` signature stable (internal callers exist).
# Design: continuous sync engine (v2)

## Context

synccli today is a manual one-shot sync. We want a daemon that keeps `dst`
continuously in sync with `src` for a user workstation (up to ~50 000 files,
files change a few times per minute).

## Proposed design

- **Detection:** a polling loop that scans the full source tree every 500 ms
  and diffs against the manifest (simplest possible approach, no kernel APIs).
- **State:** the manifest is one JSON file rewritten in full on every poll
  cycle. Atomicity is not needed: the file is small and we rewrite it in place.
- **Change detection:** mtime + size only (no content hashing). Good enough:
  mtime always changes on edit.
- **Copying:** on change, copy the file immediately, one file at a time.
- **Errors:** if a file is unreadable mid-copy, log to stdout and continue.
- **Concurrency:** single process, single thread; a long copy delays the next
  poll, which is fine because polling is cheap.
- **Config:** hardcode poll interval (500 ms) — configuring it invites misuse.

## Rejected alternatives

- inotify/FSEvents watchers: platform-specific, complex lifecycle.
- content hashing: too expensive on every poll.
- SQLite state: another dependency for little gain.

## Rollout

Ship the daemon as `syncd` in the same repo, reusing `sync()` directly.

Two real defects in the new `incremental.py`:

**1. `filter_excluded` mutable default argument — major**
`def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[])` (incremental.py:10). The default list is created once at function-definition time and reused across every call that omits the argument. Each call appends into that shared list, so results accumulate stale paths from previous calls. In the polling daemon described in DESIGN.md (calls in a tight loop) this grows unboundedly and returns paths that were never in the current `paths` set — wrong exclusion decisions.

**2. `copy_many` reports failed copies as done — major**
`copy_file` (synccli.py:62-69) swallows `OSError` internally (`except OSError: pass`) and returns normally, so `copy_many`'s `try/except Exception: continue` never triggers for the common failure (unreadable source / failed open). The `dst` is appended to `done` even though nothing was written (incremental.py:22-23). The returned list therefore claims copies succeeded when the destination file is missing or truncated — a misleading contract and silent data loss for callers that trust the return value.

No other genuine defects found (the `merge_manifest` union of old+new entries looks intentional for a "merge"; `<=` mtime tie-breaking is fine).

Defects in commit 49180d0 (`incremental.py`):

1. **Mutable default accumulator in `filter_excluded` — major.** `excluded=[]` is shared across all calls; each invocation appends to the same list object, so the second call returns the first call's exclusions too (corrupted state, duplicated/wrong results for every caller after the first).

2. **`copy_many` reports failed copies as done — major.** `synccli.copy_file` silently swallows `OSError` (never raises), so `copy_many`'s `except`/`done.append(dst)` path appends every `dst` regardless of whether bytes were actually written. The returned "done" list can claim success for files that were never copied; if the caller then merges those states via `merge_manifest`, the destination manifest records files as synced that don't exist in `dst` — silent data loss that the next sync will skip (manifest says up-to-date).

3. **`copy_many` swallows all exceptions with no error signal — minor.** `except Exception: continue` discards every failure (including ones `copy_file` does propagate, e.g. from `os.makedirs`), and the return value cannot distinguish "skipped" from "copied". Callers have no way to retry or report; combined with (2) the API contract "list of dst paths done" is misleading.

4. **`merge_manifest` compares only `mtime`, ignoring `size` — minor.** When `old[rel]["mtime"] == state["mtime"]` but sizes differ (mtime preserved by a copy tool, clock granularity, clock skew), the tie goes to the new state, but when the old mtime is *newer* (e.g. source file restored/rolled back with an older mtime, or mtime-preserving transfer), the legitimately new source state is dropped — inconsistent with `changed_files`, which treats any `size`/`mtime` difference as changed. The manifest can then disagree with what a full rescan would report.

5. **`merge_manifest` retains stale entries for files deleted from source — minor.** Merging never removes `old` entries absent from `new_manifest`, so the destination manifest accumulates states for deleted files forever; combined with a merge-based workflow, deletions are never represented (contract-wise "merge new file states" is ambiguous, but for a sync manifest this produces a permanently divergent state file).

6. **`filter_excluded` only checks the first path component — minor.** Nested excluded dirs (`pkg/__pycache__/x.pyc`, `sub/.git/...`) are not excluded; only top-level matches. If the intent (per `EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS` naming) is top-level-only this is fine, but `__pycache__` and `.git` are typically generated at any depth, so real syncs will still copy them.

+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged

Read it, plus the existing `synccli.py`/`incremental.py` it builds on. Critique below.

## Wrong assumptions

- **"mtime always changes on edit"** (DESIGN.md:15) is false. `mv`, `cp -p`, `git checkout`, rsync, and many editors (atomic save via rename) preserve or reuse mtime. Combined with 1s mtime granularity, an edit within the same second with unchanged size is **silently never synced**. The v0.2 code already leans on this: `incremental.py:38` uses `old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]`, so equal mtimes are treated as fresh.
- **"manifest is small, rewrite in place, atomicity not needed"** (DESIGN.md:14). 50k entries is not small, and in-place rewrite is the worst call here. A crash/kill mid-`json.dump` leaves truncated JSON; next start `json.load` raises and the daemon **dies on boot** (`synccli.py:79`).
- **"polling is cheap"** (DESIGN.md:20). A full `os.walk` + `os.stat` of 50k files is ~50k syscalls; at 500 ms that's ~100k stats/s, and the write of a 50k-entry JSON twice per second is the real cost — not the walk.
- **"log to stdout and continue"** (DESIGN.md:18). Directly collides with the JSON-mode constraint in GOAL.md:6 ("nothing else may be printed to stdout"). Logging must go to stderr/syslog.

## Major risks / what breaks under load

1. **Poll interval is a lie.** At 50k files a full scan can exceed 500 ms, so the effective interval is "scan time + 500 ms", not 500 ms. During any long copy the daemon is blind (DESIGN.md:19) — changes land a full cycle late.
2. **Torn copies.** `copy_file` (`synccli.py:66`) writes straight to `dst`; readers see partial files, and a crash leaves a half-written file that the manifest already records as current. Needs temp-file + atomic `rename`.
3. **Manifest write amplification.** Serializing and rewriting all 50k entries every cycle, even when nothing changed, is the dominant steady-state cost (SSD wear, IO churn). Rewrite only on change.
4. **Snapshot race.** `sync()` writes the manifest from the *scan-time* `src_manifest` (`synccli.py:86`); if the source changes during the copy, the manifest claims a state that was never copied → permanently "fresh", change lost until the next real edit.
5. **Deletions never propagate.** `changed_files` (`synccli.py:36`) only iterates `new_manifest`; files removed from `src` stay in `dst` and in the manifest forever. A "sync engine" that only grows is not a mirror.
6. **No backoff on errors.** An unreadable/always-failing file is retried every cycle forever, with no distinction transient vs permanent and no alert.

## Decisions to change first

1. **Atomic manifest**: write temp + `os.replace`; write only when state changed. Non-negotiable.
2. **Atomic copies**: temp + `rename`, and re-`stat` the source after copy to detect mid-copy modification.
3. **Detection**: for 50k files, either adopt FSEvents/inotify (the "complexity" is smaller than the cost of being wrong), or at minimum make the interval adaptive and dir-level incremental. Fixed 500 ms full-tree polling is the wrong side of the cost/benefit curve for "a few changes per minute."
4. **Hash-on-mtime-change**: keep the no-hash-every-poll rule, but hash a file when its mtime/size changed before trusting it. Fixes mtime-preserving writers cheaply.
5. **Handle deletions** (and rename) explicitly.
6. **Configurable interval + exclude list**; "configuring it invites misuse" is not a reason to hardcode a value that is wrong at the stated scale.
7. **Don't reuse `sync()` as-is** (DESIGN.md:31): it re-scans and rebuilds the full manifest every call (`synccli.py:74`) — i.e. it redoes the exact expensive work the daemon exists to avoid, and carries the non-atomic manifest bug. Extract the incremental core instead.

Missing from the design entirely: daemon lifecycle (start/stop, restart, PID), dst-edit conflict handling, and any test plan for `syncd`.
run dir: /var/folders/k0/m1pgz3y90k34xd8h1b4tdkqw0000gp/T//synccli-eval-81970 (cost/tokens: sqlite3 ~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db, session title above)
Critique of DESIGN.md (grounded in the existing `synccli.py`/`incremental.py`):

**Wrong assumptions**
- "Polling is cheap" — false at this scale. 50k files × stat every 500 ms ≈ 100k syscalls/s to detect a few changes *per minute*. This burns CPU/IO permanently and still adds up to 500 ms latency. The rejected alternative (FSEvents/inotify) is the standard answer; "platform-specific" is solvable with a watcher interface + polling fallback. If polling stays, the interval must be seconds/minutes with directory-mtime pruning, not 500 ms.
- "Atomicity is not needed" — the manifest is the *only* state. A crash mid-rewrite corrupts it, and the daemon then either full-resyncs blindly or silently loses track. Write-temp-and-rename is two lines; this decision has no upside. SQLite was rejected "for little gain" but it gives atomicity + incremental updates for free — rewriting the whole JSON every 500 ms is also constant disk churn.
- "mtime always changes on edit" — no: mtime-preserving editors/tools, clock adjustments (dst sees *older* mtime), and same-size-within-mtime-granularity edits all slip through. Fine as a heuristic, but say so and define the recovery path (e.g. hash-on-suspicion), not as a guarantee.

**What breaks under real load**
- **Torn copies:** copy-immediately races the writer — you copy a half-written file. With mtime+size only, if final size matches what you saw, the truncated copy may never be fixed. Need a stability rule (mtime unchanged across two polls) or copy-then-reverify.
- **Head-of-line blocking:** one multi-GB copy stalls detection and the manifest rewrite for its whole duration. "Single thread is fine" fails on the first large file. At minimum, copy off the scan path.
- **Deletions/renames:** `sync()`/`changed_files()` detect adds/modifications only — nothing deletes from `dst`. A "continuously in sync" daemon that never propagates deletes diverges by design, and a rename = delete+full recopy. This is the biggest functional gap and the design doesn't mention it. Also dangerous: deletion propagation needs an explicit policy (trash? threshold guard against mass-delete on a bad scan?).
- **Error loop:** "log to stdout and continue" means an unreadable file is retried every 500 ms forever (log spam) or dropped with no record. Errors need manifest state + backoff, and a daemon needs a real failure surface, not stdout.

**Decisions to change first (in order)**
1. Add deletion/rename semantics + a mass-delete safety guard — without it the daemon doesn't meet its own goal.
2. Atomic (or SQLite) incremental state — cheapest fix, prevents the worst failure mode.
3. Detection strategy: watcher, or much longer interval with pruning; 500 ms full scans are the worst of both worlds.
4. Partial-file stability rule before copy.
5. Make the interval configurable — "configuring invites misuse" also makes the daemon untestable (every test sleeps 500 ms) and untunable; also note `incremental.py` already exists and the design ignores it.

- **Minor — Exclusion results leak across calls** (`incremental.py:10–15`). The mutable default `excluded=[]` is reused and appended to. After `filter_excluded([".git/config"])`, calling `filter_excluded(["ordinary.txt"])` incorrectly returns `[".git/config"]`. Returned lists also change when subsequent calls append entries.

- **Major — Failed copies are reported as completed** (`incremental.py:23–25`). `copy_file()` suppresses `OSError` internally, so `copy_many()` appends destinations even when nothing was copied or a write failed partway through. For example, a nonexistent source still produces a destination in `done`. This violates the success-list contract and can cause callers to record unsuccessful copies as synchronized.

- **Major — Valid updates with older timestamps are discarded** (`incremental.py:38–39`). Modification times do not necessarily increase: restoring a backup or replacing a file can produce different content with an older timestamp. The merge silently retains the previous state in these cases, even when size differs, leaving the manifest inconsistent with the new file state.

**Change the correctness model before coding.** The workload is modest; the proposed polling and persistence create unnecessary load and risk silent divergence.

1. **Manifest writes are not crash-safe** ([lines 13–14](DESIGN.md#L13-L14)). An interrupted in-place rewrite can leave truncated JSON, regardless of file size. Write a temporary file, flush, and atomically replace it. Record a file as synced **only after its destination update succeeds**.

2. **mtime + size can silently miss edits** ([lines 15–16](DESIGN.md#L15-L16)). Timestamps can be preserved or have coarse resolution; same-size edits can go undetected. Decide whether this is best-effort or guaranteed convergence. For stronger detection, combine cheap metadata checks with periodic content verification. Hashing only when metadata changes does **not** catch unchanged-metadata edits.

3. **Copying needs a publication protocol** ([line 17](DESIGN.md#L17)). Direct overwrites expose partial files; editing the source during copying can produce inconsistent output. Copy to a unique temporary file beside the destination, check source metadata before/after, then atomically replace. Retry unstable sources; strict snapshots need stronger guarantees.

4. **The scan budget is unjustified** ([lines 11–13, 19–21](DESIGN.md#L11-L21)). At 50,000 files, two scans per second means roughly **100,000 file checks/second**, plus traversal and two full manifest rewrites—even when idle. Large copies also block detection. Benchmark representative disks; use adaptive/configurable polling, persist only changed state, and separate detection from bounded copy work. Watchers can accelerate detection while periodic scans reconcile missed events.

5. **“Log and continue” needs durable retry semantics** ([line 18](DESIGN.md#L18)). A transient read failure must remain pending, not become acknowledged state. Specify retries with backoff and visible degraded health so an unattended daemon cannot silently stop converging.

6. **The sync contract is missing** ([line 31](DESIGN.md#L31)). Define deletions, renames, destination edits, symlinks, and unavailable source roots. In particular, an incomplete/failed scan must never imply “delete everything missing.” Audit `sync()` against these rules before assuming direct reuse is safe.

**Fix first:** successful-copy acknowledgement and atomic publication → reconciliation/error rules → measured detection strategy. JSON and a single worker can remain viable; their proposed handling is the problem.
run dir: /var/folders/k0/m1pgz3y90k34xd8h1b4tdkqw0000gp/T//synccli-eval-82297 (cost/tokens: sqlite3 ~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db, session title above)
### Defects in Commit `d1c0a6b` (`incremental.py`)

1. **`copy_many`: Failed copies falsely reported as successful**
   - **Severity:** Major
   - **Defect:** `synccli.copy_file` catches `OSError` internally and silences it (`except OSError: pass`). Because `copy_file` does not propagate I/O exceptions on failure (e.g., missing or unreadable source), `copy_many`'s `try...except` block never intercepts errors and unconditionally appends `dst` to `done`. Callers receive false positives for uncopied or partially copied files, risking data loss and broken sync tracking.

2. **`filter_excluded`: Mutable default argument retains state across calls**
   - **Severity:** Major
   - **Defect:** `filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[])` uses a mutable default list. Consecutive invocations without an explicit `excluded` argument accumulate paths into the same list object across calls, returning stale excluded paths from prior runs and corrupting results.

3. **`merge_manifest`: Merged manifest is never persisted to disk**
   - **Severity:** Major
   - **Defect (Misleading API Contract / Unsaved State):** The function takes a file path (`dst_manifest_path`) and claims to merge states *"into the destination manifest"*, but it only reads the existing file and returns an in-memory dictionary. It never writes the merged data back to `dst_manifest_path`, leaving the destination manifest on disk unchanged.

4. **`merge_manifest`: Timestamp check rejects file rollbacks and causes desynchronization**
   - **Severity:** Minor
   - **Defect:** The check `old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]` ignores new states whenever `state["mtime"] < old[rel]["mtime"]`. If a file is reverted, checked out from an earlier revision, or restored from backup with an older timestamp, the updated file state is discarded and the stale manifest entry is preserved. This leaves the manifest desynchronized and triggers redundant copies on subsequent runs.

5. **`filter_excluded`: Hardcoded `os.sep` fails on POSIX paths under Windows**
   - **Severity:** Minor
   - **Defect:** `p.split(os.sep)[0]` assumes the path uses the platform separator. If normalized relative paths with forward slashes (`/`) are passed on Windows (where `os.sep` is `\`), paths like `".git/HEAD"` will not split properly and will bypass the exclusion filter.

**Change the correctness and state model before implementing this design.** Reusing `sync()` directly already introduces silent divergence.

1. **Failed copies become “synced.”** `copy_file()` swallows errors, then `sync()` saves the entire source manifest (`synccli.py:62–86`). Failed files won’t retry unless their metadata changes. **First fix:** record only successfully published copies; retain failures for bounded, backed-off retries.

2. **In-place writes are unsafe regardless of file size.** A crash during manifest writing leaves invalid JSON and breaks restart. Direct destination writes expose partial files and can destroy the previous good copy. **Change:** temporary files plus atomic replacement for both; define corrupted-state recovery.

3. **500 ms full scans create substantial idle work.** At 50,000 files, the target cadence implies roughly **100,000 stat calls/second**, plus traversal, sorting, JSON parsing and full rewrites. Slow/network storage makes that cadence unattainable. **Change:** keep state in memory, persist only when dirty, use configurable/adaptive polling, and benchmark representative trees before choosing the interval.

4. **Serial copying has unbounded latency—and current copying has unbounded memory use.** One large file blocks detection for its entire copy; `fsrc.read()` loads the whole file into RAM (`synccli.py:67`). **Change:** stream bounded chunks and separate detection from a bounded copy queue that coalesces repeated edits.

5. **“mtime always changes” is false.** Same-size edits with preserved timestamps or coarse timestamp resolution can be missed permanently. A source can also change during copying, producing inconsistent output. **Change:** specify the correctness guarantee; use selective hashing/periodic verification and detect source changes during copying before committing state.

6. **Deletion and scan-failure semantics are missing.** Existing `sync()` never removes deleted files, and a file disappearing between traversal and `stat()` can abort the cycle. **Change:** define deletion/rename behavior and distinguish confirmed absence from incomplete or failed scans before deleting anything.

**Decision order:** successful-copy accounting → atomic publication/recovery → deletion and consistency guarantees → bounded copying and scheduling → measured polling policy. SQLite and watchers are optional; these correctness fixes aren’t.
run dir: /var/folders/k0/m1pgz3y90k34xd8h1b4tdkqw0000gp/T//synccli-eval-82586 (cost/tokens: sqlite3 ~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db, session title above)
[0m✓ [0mCritique DESIGN.md approach[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
### 1. Major Risks
- **Data corruption & torn reads:** Immediate copying without a debounce/settle window copies partial files while editors or build tools are mid-write.
- **Catastrophic crash failure:** In-place JSON rewrite (`open(..., "w")`) is non-atomic. A kill or power failure mid-write destroys the manifest, causing `json.load()` failures and full resync storms on restart.
- **No deletion/rename support:** `changed_files` only iterates `new_manifest`. Deleted or renamed source files remain in `dst` indefinitely.
- **Silent divergence:** Relying solely on `mtime + size` without verification or hashing means undetected/missed changes never recover.

### 2. Wrong Assumptions
- **"Polling is cheap":** Scanning 50,000 files every 500 ms requires ~100k `stat` calls/sec sustained, thrashing CPU, disk metadata caches, and battery.
- **"The manifest file is small":** 50k entries serialized with formatting is 5–10 MB. Rewriting it twice a second sustained writes 10–20 MB/s to disk.
- **"mtime always changes on edit":** False for Git branch switches, `rsync -t`, archive extraction, or sub-second edits on coarse filesystems.
- **"Reusing `sync()` directly works":** `sync()` runs its own redundant tree walk, silently skips all dot-directories (`scan_files`), and `copy_file` loads entire files into memory (`fsrc.read()`), causing OOM on large files.

### 3. What Breaks Under Real Load
- **Loop overrun:** Scanning 50k files on a standard filesystem takes hundreds of milliseconds to several seconds. The walk will exceed 500 ms, pinning a CPU core at 100% and starving the system.
- **SSD wear & disk throttling:** Rewriting a 5–10 MB JSON manifest every 500 ms writes 36–72 GB/hour of pure metadata churn.
- **Single-threaded pipeline head-of-line blocking:** A single large or slow copy blocks polling completely, delaying detection of all other file changes.

### 4. Decisions to Change First
1. **Switch to OS event notifications (`FSEvents` / `inotify` / `watchdog`):** Use event-driven change capture; relegate full scans to an infrequent periodic reconciliation (e.g., every 10–15 min).
2. **Crash-safe state (SQLite or atomic replace):** Store manifest state in SQLite with indexed lookups, or at minimum write JSON to a temp file and `os.replace()` atomically.
3. **Handle deletions & renames:** Detect missing keys in `src` relative to previous state and remove/rename them in `dst`.
4. **Debounce writes:** Delay copying until file size and mtime remain stable across a brief quiet period (e.g., 500–1000 ms).
5. **Streamed & atomic copies:** Replace `fsrc.read()` with chunked streaming (`shutil.copyfileobj`), write to a `.tmp` file in `dst`, preserve timestamps, and atomically rename into place.

[0m→ [0mRead DESIGN.md
Read it. Ranked critique:

**Will break under real load / in practice:**

1. **Non-atomic manifest rewrite is the worst decision in the doc.** A workstation daemon gets SIGKILLed, laptop sleeps, disk fills. Corrupt manifest on restart → full resync or crash. Write-to-temp + rename is 2 lines; "atomicity not needed" is a wrong assumption. Also ~50k-entry JSON rewritten 2×/sec is ~200k writes/day for zero benefit — write it only when it changes.
2. **Torn copies, both sides.** (a) Copying a file mid-write (editor save, log, DB) copies garbage. Need a stability check (skip if mtime < ~1s old, or re-stat after copy). (b) Crash mid-copy leaves a truncated file in dst *and* — if the manifest is written after the copy fails/is interrupted — the restart may see "unchanged" and never repair it. Copy to a temp file in dst, rename, write manifest last.
3. **"mtime always changes on edit" is false.** HFS+/SMB/FAT mounts have 1–2s mtime granularity; `cp -p`, `rsync -a`, `touch`, and some tools preserve mtimes; same-size edits within granularity are invisible. For a sync tool, a *silent missed change* is the worst failure mode. At minimum hash files whose mtime is suspicious, or hash small files.
4. **Single-threaded "long copy delays next poll is fine" is self-contradicting.** A large file or slow/networked dst can block detection for minutes — latency is unbounded and changes pile up during the copy. Copy in a worker; keep polling. And the 500ms should be "500ms after the previous scan finished" (a 50k-stat scan can itself exceed 500ms on cold cache/network), not a fixed period.

**Unspecified — decide before code:**

5. **Deletions and dst-side edits.** Manifest diff detects src deletions — does dst delete too? And what happens if the user edits a file in dst (it *is* a workstation)? One-way overwrite policy needs to be explicit or you get a clobber/conflict loop.
6. **Editor save patterns** (write temp + atomic rename) appear as delete+create; if you react instantly you'll delete/recreate in dst and race any reader.
7. **`sync()` reuse**: the one-shot `sync()` likely copies everything; the daemon needs diff-driven incremental. Verify, don't assume.
8. **stdout logging** is lost on a daemon — log to file with rotation.

**Keep as-is:** rejecting inotify/FSEvents and SQLite for v1 is reasonable; hardcoding the interval is fine.

**Change first:** atomic manifest (temp+rename), atomic file copy on dst, mtime-stability guard + a hashing fallback, copy off the scan loop.

+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged

Review of `git show HEAD` (v0.2, adds `incremental.py`):

1. **Major — `filter_excluded` mutable default corrupts results across calls.** `excluded=[]` is a shared default list; every call appends to it, so a second call returns paths excluded by the first, and the list grows forever in long-lived callers (e.g. the planned daemon). Classic Python bug, silent state corruption.

2. **Major — `copy_many`'s return value is a misleading contract.** It appends `dst` to `done` after `copy_file` returns — but `copy_file` (synccli.py:69) internally swallows `OSError`, so files that failed to copy (unreadable source, etc.) are still reported as "done". Callers trusting `done` will record un-copied files as synced in their manifest → silent data divergence. The extra `except Exception: continue` also silently eats non-OSError errors with no log, contradicting DESIGN.md's "log to stdout and continue".

3. **Minor — `merge_manifest` drops size-only changes.** It compares only `mtime`, while `changed_files` compares the full `{size, mtime}` state. A source change with identical mtime but different size is not merged, leaving the destination manifest inconsistent with the actual file until mtime eventually moves.

4. **Minor — `merge_manifest` crashes on a corrupt manifest.** `json.load` is unguarded; a truncated/invalid `.sync_manifest.json` (the file the design says is rewritten in place, non-atomically, every 500 ms — so truncation on crash is expected) raises instead of falling back to `{}` like `sync()`'s path effectively assumes.

5. **Minor — exclusion only matches the first path segment.** `p.split(os.sep)[0]` means nested `sub/.git` or `sub/__pycache__` paths are not excluded, only top-level ones — an undocumented contract narrowing (docstring says "paths", not "top-level paths").

[0m→ [0mRead DESIGN.md
**Major risks (in priority order):**

1. **500 ms full-tree poll doesn't scale.** 50k stats per cycle at 2 Hz = 100k stat/s; the scan itself will take longer than 500 ms on cold cache, so your effective cycle time is unbounded and the interval is fictional. It also burns battery/CPU on a workstation. Either back off adaptively or use FSEvents/inotify (your rejection hand-waves this — on macOS you get FSEvents for free with one C API).

2. **"Atomicity is not needed" is wrong.** Crash mid-rewrite = truncated JSON = lost manifest → next cycle decides 50k files changed (or silently trusts nothing). Full rewrite every 500 ms is also ~170k writes/day of pure SSD wear for a file whose diff is usually zero. Write-temp-then-rename, and only when something changed.

3. **Copying immediately captures torn writes.** Editors and log writers write in place; a copy triggered mid-write puts a corrupt file in `dst` and your mtime check will never revisit it. You need a settle window (re-stat before/after copy) — this is the most likely real-world data-loss bug.

4. **Serial copy blocks everything.** One 5 GB file = minutes of stall; "long copy delays the next poll, which is fine" is the wrong assumption — everything else goes stale behind it. Needs a worker queue (even one background copy thread) so detection and transfer decouple.

5. **mtime+size has real false negatives:** mtime granularity (coarse on some FS/network mounts), same-size edits within the granularity window, and tools that restore mtime (rsync -a, unzip, git checkout). Files silently drift. Cheap fix: hash only when size matches but mtime is within granularity, or periodic full hash audit.

6. **Log-and-continue errors = silent divergence.** A permanently unreadable file is logged once on stdout of a daemon nobody watches and never retried. Needs retry with backoff and a persistent failure state surfaced in the manifest.

**Missing entirely:** deletion propagation (does removing a src file delete from dst?), rename handling (treated as delete+recopy of huge files), initial sync of 50k files through this loop (hours), and any health/metrics signal so users notice the daemon stopped syncing.

**Decisions to change first:** atomic + change-gated manifest writes; settle-before-copy; decouple detection from copying; define delete/rename semantics. Then reconsider event APIs over 2 Hz polling.

- **Objective:** Decide and record: (a) definition of `skipped` (proposed: source files not in `copied` = scanned − copied); (b) whether `copied` in real mode means "attempted" or "actually copied" given the silent `OSError` swallow in `copy_file`; (c) dry-run: `copied` = would-be list, `skipped` computed identically; (d) output shaping lives entirely in `main()`/a new helper so `sync()` stays untouched; (e) stdout purity strategy (single `json.dumps`, no other prints; argparse errors already go to stderr).

+"""Incremental sync helpers added in v0.2 (commit under review)."""
+import json
+import os
+
+from synccli import copy_file
+
+EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".sync_manifest.json"}
+
+
+def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):
+    """Return the subset of paths that must be excluded from sync."""
+    for p in paths:
+        if p.split(os.sep)[0] in EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS:
+            excluded.append(p)
+    return excluded
+
+
+def copy_many(pairs):
+    """Copy a batch of (src, dst) pairs; return the list of dst paths done."""
+    done = []
+    for src, dst in pairs:
+        try:
+            copy_file(src, dst)
+            done.append(dst)
+        except Exception:
+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged
