Review of commit 5658c2d (`incremental.py`, new file — no callers or tests yet, so all defects are latent in the new API surface):

**Major**

1. **`filter_excluded` — mutable default accumulates state across calls** (incremental.py:9). `excluded=[]` is created once at function definition. A second call with a fresh path list returns (and further mutates) the *same* list, so results from previous calls leak into every later result — corrupted exclusion set. It also mutates any caller-supplied `excluded` list in place.

2. **`copy_many` — reports failed copies as "done"** (incremental.py:17-26). `synccli.copy_file` internally swallows `OSError` (synccli.py:68-69), including mid-copy failures where `dst` has already been opened `"wb"` and truncated/partially written. In that case no exception reaches `copy_many`, so `done.append(dst)` executes for a **failed or corrupted** copy. A caller trusting the return value records those files as synced (e.g. in the merged manifest) — silent corrupted state on disk. The added bare `except Exception: continue` also hides every other failure with no log, contrary to the repo's stated error policy (DESIGN.md:18).

**Minor**

3. **`merge_manifest` drops newer states when mtime decreases** (incremental.py:37). It compares only `mtime`, ignoring `size`, while the repo's own change detection (`changed_files`) uses size+mtime. A file restored with a preserved older mtime (tar extraction, `rsync -a`, `cp -p` from backup) is treated as stale and its new state is discarded — the source change then never propagates (silent permanent divergence).

4. **`merge_manifest` — misleading contract: never writes the manifest** (incremental.py:31-39). Docstring says "Merge new file states *into the destination manifest*" and the function takes the manifest *path* and does I/O on it, but it only returns a dict; a caller reading the contract naturally assumes the on-disk manifest was updated, leaving stale state behind.

5. **`merge_manifest` — no robustness on existing manifest**: an unhandled `json.JSONDecodeError` on a corrupt/truncated manifest, and `KeyError`/`TypeError` if an entry lacks `"mtime"` (format drift), crash the merge instead of degrading to the new manifest.

6. **`filter_excluded` — name/docstring contradiction**: the name reads as "return the paths *after filtering out* excluded ones" (the keep-set), but it returns the excluded subset. A caller coding to the name syncs exactly the wrong files (.git, __pycache__). The docstring disambiguates, hence minor.

Read DESIGN.md plus the actual `sync()` it plans to reuse. Critique:

## Wrong assumptions (the math doesn't work)

- **"Polling is cheap" at 50k files is false.** `scan_files` does a full `os.walk` + sort + 50k `stat` per cycle (synccli.py:10-33). Realistically 2–10 s per cycle on a workstation, not 500 ms. The 500 ms interval is aspirational; effective latency will be many seconds and the doc's budget is already blown before copying anything.
- **Full manifest rewrite per cycle doesn't scale.** 50k entries with `indent=2` is a multi-MB JSON, `json.load`ed + dumped every cycle — significant CPU and constant SSD churn. Rewrite only when something changed.

## Correctness bugs baked into the design

- **"Atomicity is not needed" is wrong.** `open(path, "w")` truncates then writes; crash or a concurrent `synccli` run mid-write leaves a corrupt manifest. Next cycle `json.load` throws — daemon dies — or worse, empty manifest → full 50k-file recopy. Write temp + `os.replace`.
- **Failed copies are marked synced forever.** `copy_file` swallows `OSError` (synccli.py:69) and `sync()` then writes the manifest as if the copy succeeded. The comment "next pass will retry" is false. "Log and continue" (DESIGN.md:18) repeats this: unreadable files must be *excluded from the manifest update* so they retry.
- **Deletions never propagate.** `changed_files` only iterates `new_manifest` keys; stale dst files are never removed. (`collect_stale` exists but is dead code.) A sync daemon that never deletes diverges permanently.
- **Torn copies of in-flight files.** With mtime+size, a file being written by an editor looks "changed" each poll; you copy partial content. Need debounce (copy only after state is stable across 2 consecutive scans) — cheap with mtime, no hashing required.

## What breaks under real load

- **`fsrc.read()` loads the whole file into RAM** (synccli.py:67). One 10 GB VM image or log file = 10 GB RSS. Chunked copy is mandatory.
- **Single thread couples detection latency to copy time.** One big copy stalls detection of everything else for minutes. Copy queue + worker thread (detection stays single-threaded, state stays simple).
- **mtime isn't always trustworthy**: same-size writes with preserved mtime (git operations, some tools) go undetected. Acceptable, but a periodic (hourly) content-hash pass over a sampled subset would bound divergence.

## Decisions to change first

1. **Detection**: directory-mtime pruning (only stat files under dirs whose own mtime changed since last scan) — makes polling actually cheap at 50k files without touching kernel APIs. Better: `watchdog` (one dep, cross-platform) since FSEvents is the right tool on the stated platform.
2. **Manifest**: temp+rename, rewrite only on change, and merge incrementally — `incremental.py:merge_manifest` already exists and is unused; "reusing `sync()` directly" understates the refactor.
3. **Error handling**: no manifest update for failed copies; retry with backoff; log to stderr/file with timestamps, not stdout.
4. **Copies**: chunked, debounced, off the poll thread.
5. **Deletion propagation** as an explicit design decision (it's currently implicit "never").

### 1. `copy_many`: Failed file copies falsely reported as successful
- **Severity:** Major
- **Defect:** `synccli.copy_file` catches `OSError` internally and silences it (`except OSError: pass`). As a result, when a copy fails (e.g. unreadable source, permission error), `copy_file` returns `None` without raising. `copy_many`'s `try...except` never catches the failure and unconditionally appends `dst` to `done`, falsely reporting failed or skipped copies as completed.

### 2. `filter_excluded`: Mutable default argument retains state across calls
- **Severity:** Major
- **Defect:** `def filter_excluded(paths, excluded=[]):` uses a mutable default list. Calls relying on the default parameter mutate and return the same list instance across invocations. Subsequent calls accumulate paths from previous runs, corrupting results.

### 3. `merge_manifest`: Misleading API contract / does not persist to disk
- **Severity:** Major
- **Defect:** The function accepts `dst_manifest_path` and documents that it will *"Merge new file states into the destination manifest"*, but it only returns an in-memory dictionary and never writes the merged manifest back to `dst_manifest_path`. Callers expecting the manifest file on disk to be updated will leave the destination manifest stale.

### 4. `merge_manifest`: Drops updates for files with older timestamps
- **Severity:** Major
- **Defect:** The condition `old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]` ignores any update where the source file has an older timestamp (e.g. file rollback, git checkout, restored backup). The old manifest entry is retained, preventing convergence and causing future sync passes to repeatedly detect the file as changed without ever updating the manifest.

### 5. `filter_excluded`: Subdirectory `__pycache__` and POSIX path separators not handled
- **Severity:** Minor
- **Defect:** Checking only `p.split(os.sep)[0]` means `__pycache__` directories in subpackages (the standard Python location) are never matched or excluded. Furthermore, on platforms where `os.sep` is `\` (Windows), paths containing `/` separators are not split and will fail to match `EXCLUDE_TOP_DIRS`.

[0m• [0mCritique continuous sync design[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
[0m✓ [0mCritique continuous sync design[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
### 1. Major Risks

- **Inverted Scan vs. Poll Budget:** At 50,000 files, an `os.walk` + `os.stat` pass takes ~1.2–1.5s on local SSD. A 500ms full-tree poll budget is exceeded by >2× before copying any data. The loop will run continuously at 100% CPU with zero idle time.
- **Silent Data Loss on Copy Failures:** Existing `sync()` swallows `OSError` in `copy_file` and writes the full source manifest unconditionally. Any permission failure, vanished file, or disk-full error permanently records the file as synced in the manifest and is never retried. Reusing `sync()` directly inherits this data loss.
- **Corrupted Manifest on Crash:** Full in-place JSON rewrite without atomic temp-file replacement (`os.replace`) will corrupt the manifest if killed or crashed mid-write, forcing either boot failure or a full 50,000-file re-sync.
- **Torn Reads & Permanent Destination Corruption:** Copying while a file is being actively written copies partial bytes. If writing stops, the manifest already recorded the new `mtime`/`size`, so the partial copy is never repaired.
- **Unhandled FS Races in Scanner:** `os.stat` in `build_manifest` lacks `try/except` for `FileNotFoundError`/`PermissionError` (e.g., dangling symlinks or transient files deleted between `walk` and `stat`), terminating the daemon abruptly.
- **Deletions Ignored:** Only `new_manifest` entries are scanned; file deletions in `src` never propagate to `dst`.

---

### 2. Wrong Assumptions

- **"Polling is cheap":** False at 50k scale (~1.3s scan + ~4 MB JSON serialization per cycle = gigabytes of disk churn/hour just for the manifest).
- **"mtime always changes on edit":** False for archive extractions (`tar -x`), git checkouts, tools using `touch -r` / `utime`, or fast same-size edits within filesystem timestamp resolution.
- **"Atomicity is not needed because the file is small":** False. A 4 MB JSON payload is not atomic on POSIX filesystems without atomic rename semantics.
- **"Single thread delays are fine":** A large multi-gigabyte copy stalls detection for the entire tree for minutes.
- **"Hardcoded 500ms prevents misuse":** Hardcoding a rate that is mathematically impossible to sustain guarantees immediate failure under real trees.

---

### 3. What Will Break Under Real Load

- **50,000 files:** Continuous 100% CPU utilization, heavy battery drain, high SSD write amplification (~10 GB/hr rewritten for the manifest).
- **Concurrent writes:** Partial/corrupt files copied to `dst` without any reconciliation or hash validation.
- **Persistent errors:** Files with unreadable permissions will spam stdout at 2 Hz indefinitely.
- **Multiple processes:** Lack of a PID/flock file means running `syncd` twice will cause concurrent writes and race-corrupt the manifest.

---

### 4. Decisions to Change First

1. **Replace 500ms Full Polling:** Use OS file-system watchers (`inotify`/`FSEvents` via stdlib/minimal layer) with a slow fallback reconciliation sweep (e.g., every 5–15 minutes). If watchers are rejected, implement adaptive polling with exponential backoff on idle (30s+), never a 500ms full scan.
2. **Atomic State Storage:** Replace the in-place JSON rewrite with `write-to-temp + os.replace`. Better: use Python’s built-in `sqlite3` (stdlib, zero external deps) to commit entries individually upon *verified copy completion*.
3. **Advance Manifest Only on Confirmed Copy:** Never record an entry as synced unless `copy_file` succeeds.
4. **Prevent Torn Reads:** Copy to a destination `.tmp` file and verify `src` stat (`mtime` + `size`) after copy before renaming to target path.
5. **Handle FS Errors & Deletions:** Catch transient `OSError`/`FileNotFoundError` during scanning and implement tombstone/deletion propagation.
6. **Configurable & Safe Defaults:** Add process locking (`fcntl.flock`), graceful signal handling (`SIGTERM`/`SIGINT`), and rate-limited logging.

+            continue
+    return done
+
+
+def merge_manifest(dst_manifest_path, new_manifest):
+    """Merge new file states into the destination manifest."""
+    old = {}
+    if os.path.exists(dst_manifest_path):
+        with open(dst_manifest_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
+            old = json.load(f)
+    merged = dict(old)
+    for rel, state in new_manifest.items():
+        if rel not in old or old[rel]["mtime"] <= state["mtime"]:
+            merged[rel] = state
+    return merged

- **Major — `incremental.py:23–24`: Failed copies are reported as completed.** `copy_file()` suppresses `OSError`, so a missing or unreadable source returns normally and `copy_many()` adds its destination to `done`, even when nothing was copied. This violates the documented return contract and can cause callers to mark failed transfers as synced.

- **Minor — `incremental.py:10–15`: Exclusion results leak between calls.** The default `excluded=[]` is shared and returned directly. After `filter_excluded([".git/config"])`, calling `filter_excluded(["a.txt"])` incorrectly returns `[".git/config"]`. Previously returned results also change when subsequent calls append exclusions.

- **Minor — `incremental.py:38–39`: Older timestamps prevent legitimate manifest updates.** Replacing or restoring a file can change its size/content while decreasing its mtime. For example, an old state `{size: 10, mtime: 100}` merged with `{size: 20, mtime: 90}` retains the old state. Timestamp ordering is not a reliable indication of which observation is current, so the returned manifest can misrepresent the file.

[0m• [0mCritique sync daemon design[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
[0m✓ [0mCritique sync daemon design[90m Cheap-Design-Review Agent[0m
**Revise this before implementation.** The design assumes cheap scans, reliable timestamps, and harmless interrupted writes; none is a safe foundation.

### Major risks, in priority order

1. **Idle overhead scales with the entire tree** ([lines 11–14](DESIGN.md#L11-L14)). At 50,000 files, two scans/second target roughly **100,000 metadata checks/second**, plus full JSON serialization and writes even when nothing changes. Cold caches, network mounts, and competing disk activity will stretch cycles. Benchmark representative filesystems before choosing an interval.

2. **In-place manifest writes are crash-unsafe** ([lines 13–14](DESIGN.md#L13-L14)). Small files can also be truncated by a crash. Use temporary-file replacement with appropriate durability, or transactional state. Define recovery from missing/corrupt state.

3. **mtime + size can miss changes permanently** ([lines 15–16](DESIGN.md#L15-L16)). Same-size edits with preserved or coarse timestamps are concrete counterexamples. Define the correctness guarantee and use periodic content verification if those misses are unacceptable. **Hashing only when metadata changes does not fix this blind spot.**

4. **Copying blocks detection and can publish partial content** ([lines 17–20](DESIGN.md#L17-L20)). A large copy stops observation of every other file; a source changing during copying can produce inconsistent output. Separate detection from a bounded, coalescing work queue. Copy to a destination temporary file, validate source stability, then atomically replace the destination.

5. **“Log and continue” does not define convergence** ([line 18](DESIGN.md#L18)). If a failed copy advances the manifest, it may never retry; if it does not, repeated polling may hammer the same failure. Record successful application separately from observation, retain pending work, and retry with backoff and visible failure status.

6. **Mirror semantics and reuse are underspecified** ([line 31](DESIGN.md#L31)). Specify deletions, renames, symlinks, destination edits, and inaccessible source directories. An incomplete scan must not be mistaken for mass deletion. Audit `sync()` for those guarantees before making it the daemon’s core.

### Decisions to change first

- Use **watchers plus periodic reconciliation**, or measured, adaptive polling with configurable bounds.
- Choose **crash-safe state and atomic destination publication**.
- Define **retry, consistency, and deletion semantics**, then decide what can safely be reused.

SQLite is optional; crash safety and eventual convergence are not.
